```
1	# ArtAgents Architecture
2	
3	ArtAgents has three canonical public concepts:
4	
5	- **Orchestrators** coordinate multi-step workflows.
6	- **Executors** run concrete work.
7	- **Elements** are render/custom building blocks such as effects, animations, and transitions.
8	
9	Canonical packages and commands are first-class. `python3 -m artagents` is the
10	executable package gateway; every runnable tool is reached via
11	`python3 -m artagents [executors|orchestrators|elements] …`.
12	
13	## Onboarding Commands
14	
15	Run these from the repository root before editing:
16	
17	```bash
18	python3 -m artagents --help
19	git status --short
20	python3 -m artagents doctor
21	python3 -m artagents orchestrators list
22	python3 -m artagents executors list
23	python3 -m artagents elements list
24	python3 -m artagents setup
25	```
26	
27	`setup` is dry-run by default. `python3 -m artagents setup --apply` is the explicit local mutation path and delegates to element sync/install helpers.
28	
29	Canonical discovery commands are:
30	
31	```bash
32	python3 -m artagents orchestrators inspect builtin.hype --json
33	python3 -m artagents executors inspect builtin.render --json
34	python3 -m artagents elements inspect effects text-card --json
35	```
36	
37	These JSON commands are the runtime index for agents. Folder-backed
38	orchestrators and executors include metadata such as `orchestrator_root`,
39	`executor_root`, and `stage_file`; agents should load the top-level ArtAgents
40	skill first, then open only the specific folder-level `STAGE.md` needed for the
41	selected registry item. Do not package every executor and orchestrator stage
42	into one merged runtime prompt.
43	
44	Default orchestrators include `builtin.hype`, `builtin.event_talks`, and `builtin.thumbnail_maker`. Default executors include every `STEP_ORDER` built-in, upload/action executors, `builtin.understand` (audio/visual/video dispatcher), `builtin.generate_image` (with a `saint-peter-of-banodoco` onboarding preset), Moirae, and VibeComfy. Default elements include bundled effects, animations, and transitions that can be synced into `.artagents/elements/managed` and forked into `.artagents/elements/overrides`.
45	
46	Each runnable orchestrator has exactly one canonical implementation location:
47	`artagents/orchestrators/<slug>/{orchestrator.yaml,STAGE.md,run.py}` with
48	optional local `src/` modules. Each runnable executor has exactly one canonical
49	implementation location:
50	`artagents/executors/<slug>/{executor.yaml,STAGE.md,run.py}` with optional local
51	`src/` modules. Top-level `artagents/*.py` modules are shared libraries or
52	system commands only; they are not alternate executor or orchestrator
53	implementations.
54	
55	For creation decisions, use `docs/creating-tools.md` and the templates under
56	`docs/templates/`. Add an executor for one concrete action, an orchestrator for
57	a workflow, and an element for a reusable render primitive. Agents should avoid
58	manual chains of low-level stage artifacts unless they are debugging a specific
59	executor.
60	
61	## Orchestrators
62	
63	| Module or entry point | Classification | Notes |
64	| --- | --- | --- |
65	| `python3 -m artagents`, `artagents/__main__.py` | System entry point | Executable package gateway for all canonical commands. |
66	| `artagents/pipeline.py` | System command and dispatcher | Cache-aware hype command support and source of `STEP_ORDER`; not a second orchestrator format. |
67	| `artagents/orchestrators/hype` | Orchestrator | Canonical built-in hype orchestrator folder. |
68	| `artagents/orchestrators/event_talks` | Orchestrator | Canonical event-talk discovery and rendering workflow folder. |
69	| `artagents/orchestrators/thumbnail_maker` | Orchestrator | Canonical source-evidence thumbnail workflow folder. |
70	| `artagents/orchestrators/*` | Orchestrator canonical package | Folderized orchestrator manifests, registry, runner, and CLI. |
71	
72	## Executors
73	
74	Every runnable tool is a built-in or external executor exposed from exactly one canonical folder under `artagents/executors/<slug>/`.
75	
76	| Executor group | Canonical location | Notes |
77	| --- | --- | --- |
78	| Hype pipeline stages | `artagents/executors/{transcribe,scenes,quality_zones,shots,triage,scene_describe,quote_scout,pool_build,pool_merge,arrange,cut,refine,render,editor_review,validate}` | `STEP_ORDER` stages used by the hype orchestrator. |
79	| Understanding tools | `artagents/executors/{audio_understand,visual_understand,video_understand,understand}` | Concrete media understanding tools, plus a thin `understand` dispatcher executor that selects modality via `--mode`. |
80	| Standalone/service tools | `artagents/executors/{asset_cache,boundary_candidates,generate_image,human_notes,inspect_cut,open_in_reigh,publish,reigh_data,sprite_sheet,upload_youtube}` | Standalone executor capabilities. |
81	| External tools | `artagents/executors/{vibecomfy,moirae}` | VibeComfy and Moirae are external executors only, not orchestrators. |
82	
83	Executor-owned complexity stays in the executor folder, usually under optional local `src/` modules. Shared pure hype/editing logic belongs in `artagents/domains/hype`; generic plumbing belongs in `artagents/utilities`.
84	
85	## Element Support
86	
87	| Module or path | Classification | Notes |
88	| --- | --- | --- |
89	| `artagents/elements/schema.py` | Element support | Element schema and dependency metadata. |
90	| `artagents/elements/registry.py` | Element support | Deterministic resolution: active theme, overrides, managed, bundled. |
91	| `artagents/elements/bundled/{effects,animations,transitions}` | Element support | Bundled default managed elements. |
92	| `.artagents/elements/overrides` | Element support | User-editable fork target for defaults and custom elements. |
93	| `.artagents/elements/managed` | Element support | Installed managed elements that should not be overwritten by user overrides. |
94	| `artagents/elements/catalog.py` | Element support | Effect, animation, and transition catalog support used by render validation. |
95	| `scripts/gen_effect_registry.py` | Element support | Generates Remotion registries from element source roots. |
96	| `artagents/timeline.py` | Shared library and element validator | Reigh-compatible timeline schema and effect/animation/transition validation. |
97	| `remotion/*` | Element runtime support | TypeScript renderer consuming generated element registries. |
98	
99	## Shared Libraries
100	
101	| Module or package | Classification | Notes |
102	| --- | --- | --- |
103	| `artagents/contracts/*` | Shared library | Common schema dataclasses for ports, outputs, cache, commands, and isolation. |
104	| `artagents/domains/hype/*` | Domain library | Shared hype-cut/editing concepts such as arrangement rules, enriched arrangements, and text matching. |
105	| `artagents/utilities/llm_clients.py` | Utility library | Generic LLM client construction and environment handling. |
106	| `artagents/audit/*` | Shared library | Run-local provenance ledger, graph, and HTML report. |
107	| `artagents/theme_schema.py` | Shared library | Theme schema validation helpers. |
108	| `artagents/_paths.py` | Shared library | Repository and workspace path resolution. |
109	| `artagents/executors/refine/src/reviewers/*` | Executor-owned library | Focused review heuristics used only by the refine executor. |
110	| `artagents/executors/upload_youtube/src/social_publish.py` | Executor-owned library | Social publishing client logic used by `upload.youtube`. |
111	
112	This classification keeps only retained root and bin launchers; executor-owned public metadata and entrypoints live in canonical executor folders, and orchestrator-owned public metadata and entrypoints live in canonical orchestrator folders.
113	
114	## Structure Enforcement
115	
116	`python3 -m artagents doctor` fails when canonical repository structure drifts.
117	Public executor folders under `artagents/executors/<slug>/` must include
118	`executor.yaml`, `run.py`, and `STAGE.md`. Public orchestrator folders under
119	`artagents/orchestrators/<slug>/` must include `orchestrator.yaml`, `run.py`,
120	and `STAGE.md`. Executor folders must not contain orchestrator metadata, and
121	orchestrator folders must not contain executor metadata. Legacy public package
122	directories are rejected so developers do not reintroduce removed concepts.
123	A top-level `artagents/skills/` directory is also rejected; per-stage guidance
124	lives beside the executor or orchestrator it describes.
125	
126	## Generated Files and Dirty Worktrees
127	
128	Normal generated outputs belong under `runs/` or another ignored directory. Do not commit source media, rendered videos, local dependency environments, or secrets.
129	
130	Element changes may require generated Remotion registry updates. Keep `.ts`, `.js`, `.d.ts`, and `.map` siblings synchronized in `remotion/src`, then scan for stale element aliases:
131	
132	```bash
133	python3 scripts/gen_effect_registry.py
134	rg "@workspace-|workspace-effects|workspace-animations|workspace-transitions" remotion/src scripts remotion -n
135	```
136	
137	Always inspect `git status --short` before editing. Preserve unrelated user changes, especially dirty curated executor stage files such as `artagents/executors/moirae/STAGE.md` and `artagents/executors/vibecomfy/STAGE.md`.
138

92	        refresh=bool(args.banodoco_refresh or env_config.refresh),

125	    if executor.isolation.binaries:

130	def _cmd_validate(args: argparse.Namespace, registry: ExecutorRegistry) -> int:
131	    registry.validate_all()
132	    executors = [registry.get(args.executor_id)] if args.executor_id else registry.list()
133	    missing_by_executor: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {}
134	    if args.check_binaries:
135	        from .runner import check_executor_binaries
136	
137	        for executor in executors:
138	            missing = check_executor_binaries(executor)
139	            if missing:
140	                missing_by_executor[executor.id] = missing
141	    if missing_by_executor:
142	        for executor_id, missing in missing_by_executor.items():
143	            print(f"{executor_id}: missing binaries: {', '.join(missing)}", file=sys.stderr)
144	        return 1
145	    if args.executor_id:
146	        print(f"{args.executor_id}: ok")
147	    else:
148	        print(f"{len(executors)} executor(s): ok")
149	    return 0

Excellent. Now I have enough data to provide a comprehensive answer. Let me compile the findings:

## Summary

Based on my thorough exploration of the ArtAgents codebase, here are the concrete findings for your workstream layer design:

### 1. **CLI Surface Today** (pipeline.py:66-112)
Top-level subcommands dispatched via `python3 -m artagents`:
- `executors` — list, inspect, validate, install, run
- `orchestrators` — list, inspect, validate, run
- `elements` — list, inspect, sync, fork, install, update
- `doctor` — health check
- `setup` — local environment planner
- `audit` — run-local report generation
- `publish`, `upload-youtube` — social publishing
- `reigh-data` — Reigh integration

**Where a `workstream` would slot:** Modify `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/pipeline.py:66-112` to add a `if raw and raw[0] == "workstreams"` case before the final `_hype.main()` fallback. Create `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/workstreams/cli.py` mirroring `executors/cli.py` and `orchestrators/cli.py` structure with `list`, `inspect`, `run` subcommands.

### 2. **Persistence & Hidden State** (structure.py, .gitignore)
- **`.artagents/` directory** is the canonical local state root (structure.py:71, 146):
  - `.artagents/elements/managed/` — installed managed elements
  - `.artagents/elements/overrides/` — user-forked element edits
  - `.artagents/executors/<id>/venv` — per-executor Python environments
- **`runs/` is 100% ephemeral** (.gitignore:34) — all generated artifacts ignored; no "current project" pointer exists today
- **No session/context file precedent** — no `.artagents/current_run`, `.artagents/active_workstream.json`, or equivalent

**Leverage point:** Create `.artagents/workstreams.json` (or `.artagents/workstreams/`) to track active workstream metadata without modifying the `runs/` or element patterns.

### 3. **How Users Discover Runs Today**
- **No "list past runs" command** — users manually browse `runs/` directory
- **No history, show, inspect beyond the tool catalog** — only `audit --run RUN_DIR` exists (audit/cli.py:1-26) to generate post-hoc provenance reports
- **Current workflow:** `ls runs/`, `ls runs/artagents_logo_v2/`, hand-edit briefs, re-run

**Workstream opportunity:** A `workstreams list` command listing runs grouped by parent workstream ID would be immediately useful and require minimal integration.

### 4. **Slug & Naming Conventions** (executors/runner.py:276-284)
- **`_default_brief_slug()`** (runner.py, lines 276-284):
  ```python
  def _default_brief_slug(brief: Path, out: Path) -> str:
      generic_brief_names = {"brief", "plan", "prompt"}
      return out.name if brief.stem.lower() in generic_brief_names else brief.stem
  ```
- **Run slugs are `--out` directory names**, not auto-versioned: `artagents_logo`, `artagents_logo_v2`, `artagents_logo_v3` (all manually versioned)
- **No built-in collision handling or auto-increment** — user is responsible for naming `runs/my_thing` vs `runs/my_thing_v2`
- **Slugs are lazy:** if brief is named `brief.txt`, the run slug is the `--out` directory name; otherwise brief stem is used

**Workstream design note:** Workstreams can own the versioning (`workstreams run <ws_id>` auto-increments run slugs under that workstream). No collision if each run is nested as `runs/<workstream_id>/<auto_version_slug>/`.

### 5. **`.gitignore` Coverage**
Line 34: `runs/` — completely ignored. No selective "keep-worthy runs" convention exists. All video, image, audio, and log artifacts are ignored (lines 40-45).

### 6. **Existing Test Coverage Around Runs**
Tests do not exercise end-to-end run lifecycle in a way that would break with workstream nesting:
- `test_doctor_setup.py` tests setup/plan, not runs
- `test_executor_*`, `test_orchestrator_*` tests focus on registry/validation, not `--out` directory mutation
- `test_pipeline_*.py` tests validate stage chaining and brief parsing, not run discovery or history

**Safe to nest:** Running tests confirm no code path does `runs/` scanning or `current_run` pointer lookup.

### 7. **Onboarding / Documented Workflow** (docs/creating-tools.md, architecture.md)
- **Explicit "highest-level command" guidance** (creating-tools.md:5-16): Use orchestrators, not chains of low-level executors by hand
- **Artifact resumability** (creating-tools.md:14): Pipeline stages pass file artifacts (transcripts, scenes, pools, timelines, assets) to make runs resumable — workstream nesting doesn't break this
- **No documented "current project" concept** — users manage brief/output separately per invocation
- **Brief is first-class input artifact** (architecture.md:42, creating-tools.md:101-104) — workstreams can own brief lineage as optional metadata

---

## Concrete Integration Points (Leverage Without Touching Every Tool)

1. **New CLI subcommand** — add to `/artagents/pipeline.py:66-112` (one `if` clause), create `/artagents/workstreams/cli.py` (clone from `executors/cli.py`)
2. **Metadata storage** — create `.artagents/workstreams.json` (or `.artagents/workstreams/<id>.yaml`), read/write only in workstream CLI/runner
3. **Run nesting** — change `--out runs/my_thing` to `--out runs/<workstream_id>/my_thing` *client-side* (user choice in orchestrator invocation) or auto-nest in workstream runner

5. **Brief lineage optional** — workstreams can store `parent_brief_path` in metadata; not enforced in executor/orchestrator contracts

This design passes the "no touching every tool" test because workstream nesting is purely a CLI and metadata layer above the existing `--out` directory contract.

> DEVELOPER

I have a design for a "workstream" layer in the ArtAgents toolkit. Pressure-test the abstraction itself. Don't touch code — pure design critique. ~500 words.

**Context: ArtAgents is a file-based toolkit where agents (LLM agents like Claude) and humans make creative work.** Each invocation of an executor or orchestrator produces files in `runs/<slug>/`. Today these are flat siblings with no notion of "this v3 belongs with v1." The user wants iterations toward one creative idea grouped into a "workstream" so that 6 months later you can open a finished video and see every iteration that fed it.

**The design I've landed on (summary):**

1. **Tags-not-folders.** Each run writes a `run.json` with `run_id` (ULID), `workstream_id` (ULID), `parent_run_id`, input artifacts (paths + sha256 + producer run_id), output artifacts. `runs/` stays flat.
2. **Index file** `.artagents/workstreams.json` with `{schema_version, active_workstream_id, workstreams: {id: {label, status, run_ids, ...}}}`. Single fcntl-locked file, atomic replace.
3. **Auto-attribute on every run.** Decision function: explicit override wins; else if active is open → join; else if active was archived within 48h → reopen and join; else → create new. No in-flow prompts.
4. **Lifecycle:** open → archived (explicit, or on export, or after 7d idle) → reopen on related run within 48h. Lazy: enforced on read.
5. **Single chokepoint:** wrap `run_executor()` and the orchestrator equivalent. Executors themselves don't change.
6. **Provenance in timeline:** `cut`/`render` embed `{workstream_id, workstream_label (denormalized), run_id, parent_run_id, contributing_runs: [{run_id, artifact_path, sha256}]}` into `hype.metadata.json`. `contributing_runs` is computed by hashing each input asset and matching against run records.
7. **Split / merge / attach / detach** as metadata edits.
8. **Backfill** scans existing `runs/*/` clusters by mtime gap.

**Pressure-test these specifically — be combative:**

- Is **`workstream`** the right *noun*? Does it mean anything to a creator? Better candidates: `pursuit`, `thread`, `session`, `project`, `idea`, `attempt`, `take`. What's the failure mode of each?
- Is **single-parent membership** wrong? When a run consumes assets from two prior workstreams (a remix), it has only one `workstream_id` but multiple `contributing_runs`. Is that the right asymmetry, or should runs be multi-membership?
- The **denormalized `workstream_label` in the timeline** is justified as "survives index deletion." But it also goes stale on rename. Is there a better invariant — maybe denormalize the full workstream snapshot (inputs at time of render, not just label)?
- **Auto-archive at 7d idle is arbitrary.** What if the maker is on vacation and comes back to a 30-day-old idea? Auto-reopen window catches it (48h), but a 30d gap doesn't. Is the time-based heuristic wrong-shaped — should it be activity-based (count of unrelated runs since last touch) instead of time-based?
- **`parent_run_id` is the previous run in the workstream.** That's a *temporal* edge, not a *causal* one. The actual causal edge is "this run consumed that run's outputs." Should `parent_run_id` be derived from input artifacts instead of from temporal order?
- **Backfill clusters by mtime gap** — that throws away meaning. A user might have made 10 logos one afternoon (one workstream) and 3 thumbnails the same afternoon (different workstream). What's a more honest backfill?
- **What's missing** — what concept does this design refuse to model that a creator will ask for in month 2? (Branches? Tags? Favorites? Comparisons? "This v4 is the keeper, archive the rest"?)

Be willing to say the whole frame is wrong. Don't hedge. Recommend concrete changes.

I'm designing a "workstream" layer for the ArtAgents toolkit. The toolkit is used by **LLM agents** (like Claude Code) — not humans typing commands. The agent reads tool docs (STAGE.md files), invokes `python3 -m artagents executors run <id> -- <args>`, observes output, decides what to do next.

Your job: think hard about the **agent UX**. ~500 words. No code. Pure design.

**Context I need you to absorb:**

The toolkit invokes look like:
```bash
python3 -m artagents executors run builtin.cut -- --out runs/artagents_logo_v3 --brief brief.txt
python3 -m artagents orchestrators run builtin.hype -- --out runs/myvideo --brief brief.txt
```

Each invocation produces `runs/<slug>/` with various JSON manifests. The new layer adds:
- Auto-stamped `run.json` with `workstream_id`, `parent_run_id`, input/output artifacts.
- `.artagents/workstreams.json` index with active pointer.
- New CLI: `python3 -m artagents workstream {new, list, show, archive, ...}`.
- Optional `--workstream <id|@new|@active|@none>` flag on every run command.

**The critical insight:** the agent has to *care* about workstreams for them to be useful. If the agent just runs commands without thinking about grouping, the auto-attribution will silently lump everything into one giant pile, and the killer demo ("show me every iteration that fed this video") becomes "show me every run I've ever done." That's worse than nothing.

**Pressure-test these questions — be specific:**

1. **What does the agent see when it doesn't do its job?** If the agent forgets to think about workstreams, what's the failure mode and how loud is it? Should the runner *print* the active workstream on every run ("→ workstream: artagents-logo (run 7, opened 2h ago) — `workstream new` to start fresh")? Should it warn when an idle workstream is auto-reopened? Should it warn on the *first* run of a session ("active workstream is 5 days old; new pursuit?")?

2. **What signal does the agent need to make the right call?** What can the *runner* infer from context that the agent can't be trusted to declare? Examples to consider: brief content, time gap, working directory change, parent run outputs in inputs, semantic similarity to active workstream's brief.

3. **STAGE.md guidance.** Every executor has a STAGE.md. Should the workstream concept be referenced in every STAGE.md, in one global file, or in tool descriptions emitted by `inspect`? Where does the agent learn the convention?

4. **Defaults that make doing the right thing the path of least resistance.** Auto-attribute is one. What about: every `executors run` invocation prints a one-line summary including workstream membership; every `workstream show` emits a structured block the agent can parse; `workstream new` is encouraged at the start of any new conversation.

5. **The "obvious when they don't" half.** If an agent mis-attributes (groups two unrelated ideas, or splits one idea), how does that get *surfaced*? Is there a passive `workstream doctor` that flags suspicious patterns (one ws with 50 runs in 5 mins from 4 different brief stems)? Should it run automatically?

6. **Brief as the anchor.** Briefs (`brief.txt`) are first-class inputs. Could the brief itself be the implicit workstream identity (one brief = one workstream until brief changes substantially)? What does that buy and what does it lose?

7. **The conversation boundary problem.** When a *new agent conversation* starts, the agent has no memory that a workstream is active. How does it discover this without being told? Should `workstream show @active` be the first thing every agent runs in a session? Should it be wired into the skill/SKILL.md?

8. **Cost of being wrong.** What's the worst-case mis-grouping cost? Is it irreversible, or is split/merge cheap enough that the agent can be aggressive about auto-attribute and clean up later?

Give me a sharp recommendation: what changes to my design make the **agent** consistently produce well-grouped data, and how do failures become visibly loud rather than silent?

# Pressure-test

## The noun is wrong

`workstream` is a project-management word. It belongs in a Jira backlog, not in a creator's tool. Nobody opens their laptop saying "let me return to my workstream." The failure modes:

- `pursuit` — pretentious, but accurate to the *intent*: "I am chasing this idea." Best fit.
- `thread` — already overloaded (forum threads, conversation threads, git). But: it captures the temporal-with-branches shape of actual creative work. Strong second.
- `session` — wrong, implies time-bounded sitting.
- `project` — wrong, too big; a project contains many of these.
- `idea` — too cute, and conflates the noun with the content.
- `attempt`/`take` — too small, implies a single try.

**Recommend `thread`.** It's what creators already say ("I want to pull on this thread"). The git collision is a feature: the mental model transfers.

## Single-parent membership is the original sin

You will regret this within a month. A remix that pulls from two threads is not "in" one of them — it spawns a *new* thread that *descends from* both. The asymmetry you're proposing (one `workstream_id`, many `contributing_runs`) makes the lineage queryable downward but not upward: from the remix you can find the parents, but from either parent you cannot see the remix without scanning every run's `contributing_runs`.

**Fix:** drop `workstream_id` as a scalar. A run *belongs to* whichever threads claim it, computed from the DAG of `contributing_runs`. The index stores `thread.root_run_ids` and `thread.tip_run_ids`; membership is derived. This also makes split/merge degenerate — they become re-rooting operations on a graph you already have.

## Denormalized label is the wrong invariant

You're right that label goes stale on rename, but the deeper problem is that label is the wrong thing to freeze. **Freeze the inputs, not the name.** Embed `{run_id, parent_run_ids[], contributing_runs[{path, sha256, producer_run_id}]}` in `hype.metadata.json` — *no* `workstream_id`, *no* label. The thread is reconstructible from the graph. Label is for humans, lives in the index, and is allowed to change.

## Time-based archive is wrong-shaped

Activity-based is better but still wrong. The honest signal is **semantic distance**: did the next run consume any artifact descended from this thread? If yes, the thread is alive regardless of calendar time. If no, it's dormant regardless of how recent. Time is a proxy for "the maker forgot" — but consuming an artifact *is* remembering. Use the DAG.

The 48h reopen window is a band-aid for the same mistake. Drop it.

## `parent_run_id` should be causal, not temporal

Yes. Compute it from input artifact hashes against producer `run_id`. Temporal `parent_run_id` lies during interleaved work (two threads same afternoon — your own backfill example). If a run consumes nothing from prior runs, `parent_run_ids` is empty; that's a root, which is the truth.

## Backfill by mtime gap is dishonest

Don't auto-cluster. Hash every output, build the artifact-DAG from re-running input detection across existing runs, and present *that* graph for the user to label. Connected components are honest thread candidates; mtime gaps are not.

## What you're refusing to model

**Favorites / "the keeper."** Creators do not iterate to converge — they iterate to *select*. Within month two someone will ask "which v is the one?" and you'll have nowhere to put that bit. Add `run.starred: bool` and `thread.canonical_run_id` now. It's two fields and prevents a schema migration when the user inevitably asks.

Branches are already free if you go to multi-parent DAG. Comparisons are a UI concern, not schema. Tags are the next thing after stars — defer.

## Summary

Rename to `thread`. Kill scalar `workstream_id` — membership is derived from a causal DAG of `contributing_runs`. Make `parent_run_ids` causal and plural. Replace time-based lifecycle with DAG-reachability. Backfill by hash-graph, not mtime. Add `starred` and `canonical_run_id` on day one.

> AGENT

# Agent UX for Workstreams: Design Recommendations

## The core principle

You're building for an actor whose attention budget is short and whose memory resets between conversations. Every design choice has to answer: "what makes the right action obvious enough that even a distracted agent does it?" The auto-attribute default is correct, but it has to be paired with **loud, structured, machine-parseable feedback** at every boundary, or it degenerates into a silent garbage drawer.

## 1. Make every run announce its membership

Every `executors run` and `orchestrators run` should print a structured prefix line that the agent will see in tool output:

```
[workstream] active=artagents-logo  run=#7  opened=2h ago  briefs_seen=3  runs_total=14
```

This is the single most important change. The agent literally cannot ignore it — it's in the output of every command. When the agent reads its own tool result, the workstream context is right there, free, every time. Make the prefix one line and stable so the agent can pattern-match.

## 2. Tiered warnings that escalate with suspicion

Three tiers, all printed before run output (not after — the agent's attention degrades as output grows):

- **Info** (every run): the prefix above.
- **Notice** (first run of a session, or after a `cwd` change, or after >1h gap): `NOTICE: active workstream "artagents-logo" was last touched 2h ago. If this is a new pursuit, run 'workstream new'. Continuing will attribute this run to it.`
- **Warn** (suspicious): `WARN: brief stem differs significantly from workstream's recent runs. Consider 'workstream new' or 'workstream show @active' to confirm.`

The Notice tier solves the conversation-boundary problem (#7) without requiring the agent to remember anything. The runner remembers for it.

## 3. Signals the runner can infer (so the agent doesn't have to)

The runner has more context than you'd think. Cheap, high-value signals, in priority order:

1. **Brief stem hash + fuzzy similarity** to recent briefs in active workstream. This is the strongest signal and aligns with #6.
2. **Parent run lineage**: if `--brief` or any `--in-*` arg points at a file inside `runs/<some-prior-run>/`, that prior run's workstream is the strong default. Override `@active` with this.
3. **Time gap** since last run in workstream (>4h triggers Notice; >5d triggers Warn).
4. **CWD change** since workstream was opened.
5. **Brief-stem novelty**: if the brief filename or content stem is unprecedented in this workstream, Notice.

Demote anything requiring an LLM call (semantic embeddings) to a `workstream doctor` background check, not the hot path.

## 4. Brief-as-anchor (#6) — yes, but as a *signal*, not the identity

Making brief identity == workstream identity is too rigid: agents legitimately iterate on a brief inside one pursuit, and legitimately reuse a brief across pursuits. But brief stem similarity is the **best single heuristic** for auto-suggesting `workstream new`. Treat brief as the dominant evidence in `workstream doctor`, not as the primary key.

## 5. STAGE.md and discoverability (#3)

Don't pollute every STAGE.md. Do three things instead:

- One **global `WORKSTREAMS.md`** at toolkit root, referenced from the top-level skill/SKILL.md.
- A one-line footer in `executors inspect` output: `"This run will be attributed to workstream <id> unless --workstream is set. See WORKSTREAMS.md."`
- The runtime prefix in #1 — which is the *real* documentation, because it's what the agent actually sees.

The skill should instruct: **"At the start of any session that will produce runs, call `workstream show @active` first."** That single sentence in SKILL.md is worth more than paragraphs in every STAGE.md.

## 6. `workstream show @active` as the session opener

Make it cheap, structured, and idempotent. Output should be a parseable block: id, opened-at, last-touched, run count, recent briefs (stems), recent run ids, suggested action (`continue` | `new` | `confirm`). This single command answers "what's going on?" in one tool call. Wire it into SKILL.md as the recommended first move.

## 7. `workstream doctor` — passive, automatic, loud when wrong (#5)

Run it implicitly at the end of every `workstream new` and on every `workstream list`. Surface flags like:

- 50 runs in 5 minutes from 4 distinct brief stems → likely mis-grouped, suggest split.
- Two workstreams open with near-identical brief stems → suggest merge.
- Workstream open >7d with no runs in last 5d → suggest archive.

The agent will see these flags every time it lists, which is the natural inspection point.

## 8. Make split/merge cheap (#8)

If split/merge are O(1) metadata operations (just rewriting `run.json` workstream pointers and the index), then **be aggressive about auto-attribute** and lean on `doctor` to surface mistakes. Cheap reversibility is what licenses the whole "auto-attribute by default" stance. If split/merge are expensive or lossy, the entire design above gets more conservative — make them cheap first, then enable aggressive defaults.

## The sharp recommendation

Three changes, in priority order, make the agent consistently produce well-grouped data:

1. **Print a structured `[workstream]` prefix on every run command.** Without this, nothing else matters — the agent has no in-band signal.
2. **Notice/Warn tiers triggered by runner-inferred signals (time gap, brief novelty, parent lineage, cwd).** The runner does the thinking the agent won't.
3. **`workstream show @active` as the documented first move of every session, wired into SKILL.md, plus cheap split/merge so mistakes are recoverable.**

Failures become loud because (a) every command output carries workstream state, (b) suspicious patterns trigger Warn lines the agent will read, and (c) `doctor` surfaces mis-groupings at natural inspection points. The agent doesn't need to remember workstreams exist — the toolkit reminds it on every interaction.

> DEVELOPER

Design critique, no code. ~500 words.

I'm designing a "thread" layer for the ArtAgents toolkit (file-based creative-work toolkit used by LLM agents). Each invocation produces files in `runs/<slug>/`. A thread groups runs that iterate toward one creative idea. So far the model is:

- `run` = one invocation, has `run_id`, `thread_id`, `parent_run_ids` (causal, hash-derived from input artifacts), `output_artifacts: [{path, sha256, kind}]`.
- `thread` = the pursuit. Has `run_ids`, `canonical_run_id` (the keeper).
- Auto-attribute via @active pointer + lineage inference + idle reopen window.

**Now I'm hitting a missing primitive: variants of one attempt.**

A single run can produce N siblings simultaneously: `generate_image` produces 4 candidates; `logo_ideas` orchestrator produces a grid of 8; `cut --variants 3` produces three timeline alternatives. Today these are just N files in `output_artifacts` with no notion of:
- "these N are siblings of one attempt" (a *variant group*)
- "the user picked variant 3, it became the input to the next run"
- "show me only the chosen variants in the iteration history"
- "show me all the variants of attempt #7"

**Pressure-test these design choices — be opinionated:**

1. **What's the right primitive?** Options:
   - (a) `variant_group_id` on each output artifact: cheap, but a variant_group is just a list of artifact references with no own metadata.
   - (b) Sub-runs: each variant is a child `run` of a parent attempt run. Heavyweight; doesn't match how multi-output executors actually work (one process, N files).
   - (c) An `attempt` concept that sits between `run` and `artifact`: one run can contain multiple attempts; one attempt can contain multiple variants.
   - (d) Reuse `run` itself as the variant group, and demand multi-output executors fan out to N runs (heavy, breaks existing executors).
   - Or your own.

2. **Selection semantics.** When the user (or agent) picks "variant 3 is the keeper":
   - Where does the choice live? On the variant artifact? On the run? On the thread? In a separate `selections.json`?
   - What about partial selection ("variants 2 and 4 are both keepers, going to remix them")?
   - What about *deselection* (a variant the user explicitly discarded)?
   - When the next run consumes the chosen variant, is the parentage edge automatically the run that produced it, or a stronger "I chose this over its siblings" edge?

3. **Variants vs iterations.** The failure mode I worry about: an agent runs `generate_image` 4 times sequentially with the same prompt and treats those as "4 iterations" instead of "4 variants of one attempt." How do we tell them apart? Or is the distinction not actually meaningful — should "I ran this 4 times back to back with identical-ish inputs" be auto-promoted to a variant group post-hoc?

4. **Variants of timelines.** A `cut` run that produces three alternative timelines is a variant group of timelines, each of which is itself a complex multi-clip artifact. Does that fit the same primitive, or does it need something else?

5. **Reliability.** What's the cheapest way an agent reliably groups variants without forgetting? Auto-grouping all artifacts of a single run as siblings is the default — but breaks if a run produces both a variant set AND ancillary artifacts (e.g. 4 logo candidates + 1 grid contact sheet + 1 manifest.json). How do you tell variants from ancillaries automatically?

6. **The killer view.** "Show me all variants generated for run #7, with which one was chosen and which descended from it." What's the minimum metadata to support this query in O(1) lookups, not graph traversal?

Recommend a concrete addition (or replacement) to my design. Be willing to say none of options (a)-(d) are right and propose your own.

> DEVELOPER

No code. ~500 words. Pure UX design for LLM agents using a CLI toolkit.

Context: ArtAgents is a creative-work toolkit invoked by LLM agents like Claude (`python3 -m artagents executors run <id> -- <args>`). I've designed a "thread" layer that auto-attributes every run to a creative pursuit, with a `[thread]` prefix line on every command's stdout so the agent always sees thread state in its tool output.

**Now I need to extend this for variants.** A single run often produces N siblings (4 logo candidates, 3 alternative timelines, 8 idea sketches). The agent needs to:

- Know that N artifacts of the previous run were *variants of one attempt*, not just unrelated outputs.
- Mark which variant(s) were chosen as keepers ("variant 3 is the one").
- Have the next run automatically inherit the chosen variant as its input.
- Be able to query "what variants did we make for the logo?" and "which was kept?"

**Critical: this must work without the agent remembering anything between turns.** The agent has no persistent memory across conversations and limited working memory within them.

**Pressure-test specifically:**

1. **Selection. What's the agent gesture for "variant 3 is the keeper"?**
   - Explicit: `python3 -m artagents thread select <run-id> --variant 3` or `--artifact path/to/v3.png`. Verbose but unambiguous.
   - Implicit: the next run consumes one variant via `--in <path>` → that variant is auto-marked "chosen." Cute but conflates two operations and doesn't handle "I picked it but haven't used it yet."
   - Visual: agent reads variants, writes a JSON pick file. Brittle.
   - Or your own.
   What does the agent do reliably and what does it forget?

2. **Discovery. When a run produces variants, how does the agent see them?**
   The `[thread]` prefix on the *next* run could include `last_run produced 4 variants (none chosen yet)`. Should there be a `Notice: previous run produced 4 unselected variants — pick one with 'thread select ...' or pass --variant N to your next run.`? Does that pollute, or is it the right loudness?

3. **The "ran it 4 times" failure mode.** An agent runs `generate_image` four times sequentially with similar prompts, treating them as iterations rather than as "I want 4 candidates of the same attempt." What's the UX response?
   - Detect and warn: "looks like 4 near-duplicate prompts in 2 minutes; consider --variants 4 next time"?
   - Allow post-hoc grouping: `thread variants merge <run-id> <run-id> <run-id>`?
   - Just accept it (each run is its own attempt)?

4. **Default behavior. When a multi-output executor (`generate_image -n 4`) finishes, what should the runner do automatically?**
   - Auto-mark all 4 outputs as a variant group: yes, obviously.
   - Auto-pick the "best" one: never, that's a creative judgment.
   - Auto-prompt the agent to pick: would require interactivity that doesn't fit the CLI model.
   - Print a structured `[variants] generated 4 — none chosen` line that the agent will see in tool output: my proposal.

5. **Misalignment becomes loud how?**
   - If a thread has variants from many runs and *zero selections*, that's a smell. How surfaced?
   - If the agent picks variant 3 but then passes variant 1 to the next run, that's contradiction. Auto-detect, or trust the agent?
   - If a run's "variants" are actually heterogeneous (a grid sheet + 4 candidates + a manifest), the variant group is poisoned. How does the runner figure out which outputs are siblings vs ancillaries?

6. **Iteration video implication.** The killer view is a video walkthrough: "here are the 47 runs that fed this video, including the sibling variants we considered at each step." For that to be visually coherent, the variants→selection edges need to be unambiguous. What's the minimum agent gesture per attempt to keep the iteration video honest?

7. **Ergonomic minimum.** What's the smallest set of commands/flags an agent must reliably use to get this right? If it's more than 1-2 per attempt, the agent will skip it and the data will rot.

Give me a sharp recommendation: the exact gestures, the exact runner-emitted lines, the exact failure surfacing. I'll bake whatever you recommend into my design.

> AGENT

# Variants: Design Critique

## 1. The right primitive

None of (a)-(d) as stated. The cleanest model is **(a) plus a typed role**, not a new entity. A `variant_group` is not a thing in the world — it's a *relationship among artifacts within a run*. Inventing an `attempt` table (option c) gives you a fourth noun to teach the agent, and sub-runs (b/d) lie about what executors actually do.

Concretely: extend each artifact with `role` and `group`:

```
output_artifacts: [
  {path: candidate_01.png, kind: image, role: variant, group: g1, group_index: 0},
  {path: candidate_02.png, kind: image, role: variant, group: g1, group_index: 1},
  {path: contact_sheet.png, kind: image, role: ancillary},
  {path: manifest.json,    kind: json,  role: manifest},
]
```

`group` is a stable hash (e.g. `sha256(run_id + "logo_candidates")`) so it's addressable and dedupes naturally. No separate registry needed. This solves Q5 (ancillaries) by making `role` mandatory at executor declaration time — manifests/contact-sheets are simply not `role: variant`.

## 2. Selection semantics

Selection lives at the **thread**, not the run or the artifact. The thread is what "wants something"; the artifact doesn't know it's been chosen.

A single append-only `selections.jsonl` per thread:

```
{ts, group, picked: [sha256...], discarded: [sha256...], by: "user"|"agent", note?}
```

This handles partial selection (multi-pick), deselection (explicit discards survive), and revision (later entries supersede; nothing is mutated). The "current keepers for group g" is a reduce over the log, cached.

For parentage: keep `parent_run_ids` as is (causal, hash-derived — don't pollute it), and add a separate `chosen_from_group` edge on the *consuming* run pointing at `(group, sha256)`. That preserves the distinction between "this descended from X" and "this was picked over its siblings," which you want for the killer view.

## 3. Variants vs iterations

Don't auto-promote. The distinction *is* meaningful and the heuristic is unreliable enough to corrupt history. Instead, make the agent's life easier in two ways:

- Executors that natively produce N siblings emit `role: variant` automatically — zero agent effort, the common case is free.
- For the sequential case, offer a cheap post-hoc op: `thread group <run_ids...> --as-variants`. This creates a synthetic group spanning runs. Mark it `synthesized: true` so you can tell real fan-out from retroactive grouping.

The failure you describe (4 sequential runs treated as iterations) is fine to leave as iterations until someone asserts otherwise. Iteration *is* the honest description of what happened.

## 4. Timeline variants

Same primitive, no special case. A timeline is just an artifact with `kind: timeline` whose `path` is a directory or `.edl`-like manifest. The variant group cares about sibling identity, not artifact internal complexity. If you need cross-variant diffing for timelines, that's a viewer concern, not a model concern.

## 5. Reliability

Covered above: declared `role` at the executor, not inferred. The rule is "anything not declared `role: variant` is `role: ancillary` by default." Executors opt in to variants; this fails safe.

## 6. The killer view in O(1)

Add one denormalized index file per thread: `groups.json`, mapping `group_id -> {run_id, artifact_shas[], chosen[], discarded[], descendant_run_ids[]}`. Updated on run-write and on selection-write. Both writers already touch the thread directory. The query is a single file read.

**Net recommendation:** add `role` + `group` + `group_index` to artifacts; add a thread-level `selections.jsonl`; add a `chosen_from_group` edge on consuming runs; maintain a `groups.json` index. No new top-level entity.

51	def _die(message: str) -> None:

59	
60	def _read_env_value(env_path: Path, key: str) -> str:
61	    if not env_path.is_file():
62	        return ""
63	    for raw in env_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
64	        line = raw.strip()
65	        if not line or line.startswith("#") or "=" not in line:
66	            continue
67	        if line.startswith("export "):
68	            line = line[len("export ") :].strip()
69	        env_key, env_value = line.split("=", 1)
70	        if env_key.strip() == key:
71	            return env_value.strip().strip('"').strip("'")
72	    return ""
73	
74	
75	def _candidate_env_files(env_file: Path | None) -> list[Path]:
76	    candidates: list[Path] = []
77	    if env_file is not None:
78	        candidates.append(env_file)
79	    repo_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
80	    workspace = repo_root.parent
81	    candidates.extend(
82	        [
83	            Path.cwd() / "this.env",
84	            Path.cwd() / ".env",
85	            Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "this.env",
86	            Path(__file__).resolve().parent / ".env",
87	            repo_root / "this.env",
88	            repo_root / ".env",
89	            workspace / "this.env",
90	            workspace / ".env",
91	            workspace / "reigh-app" / "this.env",
92	            workspace / "reigh-app" / ".env",
93	            workspace / "reigh-worker" / "this.env",
94	            workspace / "reigh-worker" / ".env",
95	            workspace / "reigh-worker-orchestrator" / "this.env",
96	            workspace / "reigh-worker-orchestrator" / ".env",
97	            Path.home() / "this.env",
98	            Path.home() / ".env",
99	            Path.home() / ".codex" / "this.env",
100	            Path.home() / ".codex" / ".env",
101	            Path.home() / ".claude" / "this.env",
102	            Path.home() / ".claude" / ".env",
103	            Path.home() / ".hermes" / "this.env",
104	            Path.home() / ".hermes" / ".env",
105	        ]
106	    )
107	    seen: set[Path] = set()
108	    unique: list[Path] = []
109	    for candidate in candidates:
110	        resolved = candidate.expanduser().resolve()
111	        if resolved not in seen:
112	            seen.add(resolved)
113	            unique.append(resolved)
114	    return unique

117	def load_api_key(env_file: Path | None = None) -> str:
118	    if key := os.environ.get("OPENAI_API_KEY", "").strip():
119	        return key
120	    tried: list[str] = ["OPENAI_API_KEY environment variable"]
121	    for candidate in _candidate_env_files(env_file):
122	        tried.append(str(candidate))
123	        if key := _read_env_value(candidate, "OPENAI_API_KEY"):
124	            return key
125	    raise SystemExit(f"OPENAI_API_KEY not found. Tried: {', '.join(tried)}")
126	
127	
128	def _normalize_format(value: str) -> str:
129	    fmt = value.lower()
130	    if fmt not in FORMATS:
131	        _die("--output-format must be png, jpeg, jpg, or webp")
132	    return "jpeg" if fmt == "jpg" else fmt
133	
134	
135	def _parse_size(size: str) -> tuple[int, int] | None:
136	    match = re.fullmatch(r"([1-9][0-9]*)x([1-9][0-9]*)", size)
137	    if not match:
138	        return None
139	    return int(match.group(1)), int(match.group(2))
140	
141	
142	def _validate_size(size: str, model: str) -> None:
143	    if size == "auto":
144	        return
145	    if model != "gpt-image-2":
146	        if size not in {"1024x1024", "1536x1024", "1024x1536"}:
147	            _die("For models before gpt-image-2, size must be 1024x1024, 1536x1024, 1024x1536, or auto.")
148	        return
149	    parsed = _parse_size(size)
150	    if parsed is None:
151	        _die("size must be auto or WIDTHxHEIGHT, for example 1024x1024")
152	    width, height = parsed
153	    if width % 16 or height % 16:
154	        _die("gpt-image-2 size width and height must be multiples of 16px")
155	    if max(width, height) > GPT_IMAGE_2_MAX_EDGE:
156	        _die("gpt-image-2 size maximum edge length is 3840px")
157	    if max(width, height) / min(width, height) > GPT_IMAGE_2_MAX_RATIO:
158	        _die("gpt-image-2 size long edge to short edge ratio must not exceed 3:1")
159	    pixels = width * height
160	    if pixels < GPT_IMAGE_2_MIN_PIXELS or pixels > GPT_IMAGE_2_MAX_PIXELS:
161	        _die("gpt-image-2 size total pixels must be between 655,360 and 8,294,400")
162	
163	
164	def _validate_payload(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
165	    model = str(payload["model"])
166	    if not model.startswith("gpt-image-"):
167	        _die("--model must be a GPT Image model, for example gpt-image-2")
168	    n = int(payload["n"])
169	    if n < 1 or n > 10:
170	        _die("--n must be between 1 and 10")
171	    _validate_size(str(payload["size"]), model)
172	    if payload["quality"] not in QUALITIES:
173	        _die("--quality must be low, medium, high, or auto")
174	    if payload.get("background") and payload["background"] not in BACKGROUNDS:
175	        _die("--background must be opaque, auto, or transparent")
176	    if model == "gpt-image-2" and payload.get("background") == "transparent":
177	        _die("gpt-image-2 does not support transparent backgrounds; use opaque/auto or explicitly choose an older supported model")
178	    if payload.get("moderation") and payload["moderation"] not in MODERATION:
179	        _die("--moderation must be auto or low")
180	    compression = payload.get("output_compression")
181	    if compression is not None and not (0 <= int(compression) <= 100):
182	        _die("--output-compression must be between 0 and 100")
183	
184	
185	def _normalize_job(item: Any, index: int) -> dict[str, Any]:
186	    if isinstance(item, str):
187	        prompt = item.strip()
188	        if not prompt:
189	            _die(f"Empty prompt at item {index}")
190	        return {"prompt": prompt}
191	    if isinstance(item, dict) and str(item.get("prompt", "")).strip():
192	        return dict(item)
193	    _die(f"Invalid prompt item {index}; expected string or object with prompt")
194	    return {}
195	
196	
197	def _load_prompts(path: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
198	    if not path.is_file():
199	        _die(f"Prompts file not found: {path}")
200	    text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
201	    if not text:
202	        _die(f"Prompts file is empty: {path}")
203	    if path.suffix.lower() == ".json":
204	        data = json.loads(text)
205	        if not isinstance(data, list):
206	            _die("JSON prompts file must contain an array")
207	        return [_normalize_job(item, index + 1) for index, item in enumerate(data)]
208	    jobs: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
209	    for line_no, raw in enumerate(text.splitlines(), start=1):
210	        line = raw.strip()
211	        if not line or line.startswith("#"):
212	            continue
213	        if line.startswith("{") or line.startswith('"'):
214	            jobs.append(_normalize_job(json.loads(line), line_no))
215	        else:
216	            jobs.append({"prompt": line})
217	    if not jobs:
218	        _die("No prompts found")
219	    return jobs
220	
221	
222	def _slugify(text: str) -> str:
223	    value = re.sub(r"[^a-zA-Z0-9]+", "-", text.strip().lower()).strip("-")
224	    return value[:64] or "image"
225	
226	
227	def _output_paths(out_dir: Path, prompt: str, fmt: str, job_index: int, n: int, explicit_out: str | None) -> list[Path]:
228	    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
229	    ext = "." + fmt
230	    if explicit_out:
231	        base = Path(explicit_out)
232	        if not base.suffix:
233	            base = base.with_suffix(ext)
234	        base = out_dir / base.name
235	    else:
236	        base = out_dir / f"{job_index:03d}-{_slugify(prompt)}{ext}"
237	    if n == 1:
238	        return [base]
239	    return [base.with_name(f"{base.stem}-{i}{base.suffix}") for i in range(1, n + 1)]
240	
241	
242	def _call_image_api(payload: dict[str, Any], api_key: str, timeout: int) -> dict[str, Any]:
243	    request = Request(
244	        API_URL,
245	        data=json.dumps(payload).encode("utf-8"),
246	        headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {api_key}", "Content-Type": "application/json"},
247	        method="POST",
248	    )
249	    try:
250	        with urlopen(request, timeout=timeout) as response:
251	            return json.loads(response.read().decode("utf-8"))
252	    except HTTPError as exc:
253	        detail = exc.read().decode("utf-8", errors="replace")
254	        _die(f"OpenAI API error {exc.code}: {detail}")
255	    except URLError as exc:
256	        _die(f"Network error: {exc}")
257	    return {}
258	
259	
260	def _write_images(response: dict[str, Any], paths: list[Path], force: bool) -> list[str]:
261	    written: list[str] = []
262	    for item, path in zip(response.get("data") or [], paths):
263	        image_b64 = item.get("b64_json")
264	        if not image_b64:
265	            _warn(f"No b64_json returned for {path}")
266	            continue
267	        if path.exists() and not force:
268	            _die(f"Output exists: {path} (use --force to overwrite)")
269	        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
270	        path.write_bytes(base64.b64decode(image_b64))
271	        print(f"Wrote {path}")
272	        written.append(str(path))
273	    return written
274	
275	
276	def _jobs_from_args(args: argparse.Namespace) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
277	    jobs = [{"prompt": prompt} for prompt in args.prompt or []]
278	    if args.prompts_file:
279	        jobs.extend(_load_prompts(args.prompts_file))
280	    if not jobs and args.preset:
281	        preset = PRESETS.get(args.preset)
282	        if preset is None:
283	            _die(f"Unknown preset {args.preset!r}; available: {', '.join(sorted(PRESETS))}")
284	        jobs.append({"prompt": preset["prompt"]})
285	    if not jobs:
286	        _die("Provide --prompt, --prompts-file, or --preset")
287	    return jobs
288	
289	
290	def _open_first_rendered(out_dir: Path) -> None:
291	    rendered = sorted(out_dir.glob("*.png")) + sorted(out_dir.glob("*.jpeg")) + sorted(out_dir.glob("*.webp"))
292	    if not rendered:
293	        print("No image was rendered; nothing to open.", file=sys.stderr)
294	        return
295	    target = rendered[0]
296	    print(f"Opening {target}")
297	    subprocess.run(["open", str(target)], check=False)
298	
299	
300	def generate(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
301	    jobs = _jobs_from_args(args)
302	    api_key = "" if args.dry_run else load_api_key(args.env_file)
303	    out_dir = args.out_dir
304	    default_format = _normalize_format(args.output_format)
305	    manifest: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
306	    audit = AuditContext.from_env()
307	
308	    for index, job in enumerate(jobs, start=1):
309	        prompt = str(job["prompt"]).strip()
310	        payload = {
311	            "model": job.get("model", args.model),
312	            "prompt": prompt,
313	            "n": int(job.get("n", args.n)),
314	            "size": job.get("size", args.size),
315	            "quality": job.get("quality", args.quality),
316	            "output_format": _normalize_format(str(job.get("output_format", default_format))),
317	            "output_compression": job.get("output_compression", args.output_compression),
318	            "background": job.get("background", args.background),
319	            "moderation": job.get("moderation", args.moderation),
320	        }
321	        payload = {key: value for key, value in payload.items() if value is not None}
322	        _validate_payload(payload)
323	        paths = _output_paths(out_dir, prompt, payload["output_format"], index, int(payload["n"]), job.get("out"))
324	
325	        if args.dry_run:
326	            print(json.dumps({"endpoint": API_URL, "outputs": [str(path) for path in paths], **payload}, indent=2))
327	            continue
328	
329	        print(f"[{index}/{len(jobs)}] Calling {payload['model']} for {payload['n']} image(s)", file=sys.stderr)
330	        started = time.time()
331	        response = _call_image_api(payload, api_key, args.timeout)
332	        print(f"[{index}/{len(jobs)}] Completed in {time.time() - started:.1f}s", file=sys.stderr)
333	        written = _write_images(response, paths, args.force)
334	        if audit is not None:
335	            prompt_id = audit.register_prompt_ref(
336	                prompt=prompt,
337	                label=f"Image prompt {index}",
338	                stage="generate_image",
339	                metadata={key: value for key, value in payload.items() if key != "prompt"},
340	            )
341	            output_ids = [
342	                audit.register_asset(
343	                    kind="generated_image",
344	                    path=output,
345	                    label=Path(output).name,
346	                    parents=[prompt_id],
347	                    stage="generate_image",
348	                    metadata={
349	                        "model": payload.get("model"),
350	                        "size": payload.get("size"),
351	                        "quality": payload.get("quality"),
352	                        "output_format": payload.get("output_format"),
353	                        "created": response.get("created"),
354	                    },
355	                )
356	                for output in written
357	            ]
358	            audit.register_node(
359	                stage="generate_image",
360	                label=f"Generate image job {index}",
361	                parents=[prompt_id],
362	                outputs=output_ids,
363	                metadata={"model": payload.get("model"), "n": payload.get("n"), "usage": response.get("usage")},
364	            )
365	        manifest.append(
366	            {
367	                "prompt": prompt,
368	                "request": {key: value for key, value in payload.items() if key != "prompt"},
369	                "outputs": written,
370	                "usage": response.get("usage"),
371	                "created": response.get("created"),
372	            }
373	        )
374	
375	    if args.manifest and not args.dry_run:
376	        args.manifest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
377	        args.manifest.write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
378	        if audit is not None:
379	            audit.register_asset(
380	                kind="image_manifest",
381	                path=args.manifest,
382	                label="Generated image manifest",
383	                stage="generate_image",
384	                metadata={"jobs": len(manifest)},
385	            )
386	        print(f"Wrote {args.manifest}")
387	
388	    if args.preset and not args.dry_run and not args.no_open:
389	        preset = PRESETS.get(args.preset)
390	        if preset and preset.get("open_result"):
391	            _open_first_rendered(out_dir)
392	    return 0
393	
394	
395	def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
396	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Generate images with OpenAI GPT Image models.")
397	    add = parser.add_argument
398	    add("--prompt", action="append", help="Prompt; repeat for multiple prompts.")
399	    add("--prompts-file", type=Path, help="Text, JSON, or JSONL prompt list.")
400	    add(
401	        "--preset",
402	        choices=sorted(PRESETS),
403	        help="Use a canned prompt + behaviour preset (e.g. saint-peter-of-banodoco). "
404	        "If no --prompt or --prompts-file is given, the preset's prompt is used.",
405	    )
406	    add("--no-open", action="store_true", help="Suppress opening the rendered image for presets that auto-open.")
407	    add("--model", default=DEFAULT_MODEL)
408	    add("--n", type=int, default=1, help="Images per prompt.")
409	    add("--size", default=DEFAULT_SIZE)
410	    add("--quality", default=DEFAULT_QUALITY)
411	    add("--output-format", default=DEFAULT_FORMAT)
412	    add("--output-compression", type=int)
413	    add("--background", choices=sorted(BACKGROUNDS))
414	    add("--moderation", choices=sorted(MODERATION))
415	    add("--out-dir", type=Path, default=Path("output/gpt-image"))
416	    add("--manifest", type=Path, default=Path("output/gpt-image/manifest.json"))
417	    add("--env-file", type=Path)
418	    add("--timeout", type=int, default=180)
419	    add("--force", action="store_true")
420	    add("--dry-run", action="store_true")
421	    return parser
422	
423	
424	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
425	    args = build_parser().parse_args(argv)
426	    return generate(args)
427	
428	
429	if __name__ == "__main__":
430	    raise SystemExit(main())
431

52	        required=True,
53	        help=(
54	            "Output directory. Writes hype.edl.csv, hype.timeline.json, "
55	            "hype.assets.json, and hype.metadata.json."
56	        ),
57	    )
58	    parser.add_argument("--transcript", type=Path, help="Transcript JSON used for arrangement metadata.")
59	    parser.add_argument("--shots", type=Path, help="Optional shots.json for future enrichment.")
60	    parser.add_argument("--arrangement", type=Path, help="Arrangement JSON for pool-based multitrack assembly.")
61	    parser.add_argument("--pool", type=Path, help="Pool JSON used with --arrangement.")
62	    parser.add_argument("--brief", type=Path, help="brief.txt used with --arrangement.")
63	    parser.add_argument("--theme", help="Theme id, theme directory, or path to theme.json.")
64	    parser.add_argument("--asset", action="append", default=[], help="Additional source asset mapping in KEY=PATH form.")
65	    parser.add_argument("--verbose", action="store_true", help="Print ffprobe cache activity.")
66	    parser.add_argument(
67	        "--primary-asset",
68	        help=(
69	            "Asset key that the --scenes / --transcript / --shots CLI inputs describe. "
70	            "Defaults to main when that key exists (single-source and plain-text picks). "
71	            "For multi-source JSON picks without a main key, this flag is required."
72	        ),
73	    )
74	    parser.add_argument(
75	        "--renderer",
76	        choices=["remotion"],
77	        default="remotion",
78	        help="Render backend. remotion (default) uses tools/remotion/.",
79	    )
80	    parser.add_argument("--render", action="store_true", help="Render clips and concat them into hype.mp4.")
81	    return parser

83	def load_json(path: Path) -> Any:
84	    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))

87	def _resolve_theme_path(theme_value: str | None) -> Path | None:
88	    if theme_value is None:
89	        return None
90	    candidate = Path(theme_value)
91	    if candidate.name == "theme.json":
92	        return candidate
93	    if candidate.exists() and candidate.is_dir():
94	        return candidate / "theme.json"
95	    if candidate.exists():
96	        return candidate
97	    return WORKSPACE_ROOT / "themes" / theme_value / "theme.json"
98

100	def _theme_slug_from_path(theme_path: Path | None) -> str | None:
101	    """Derive the theme slug (directory name) from a theme.json or theme dir path."""
102	    if theme_path is None:
103	        return None
104	    path = Path(theme_path)
105	    if path.name == "theme.json":
106	        return path.parent.name
107	    if path.is_dir():
108	        return path.name
109	    return path.parent.name

112	def _theme_default_clip_sec(theme: dict[str, Any] | None) -> float | None:
113	    pacing = theme.get("pacing") if isinstance(theme, dict) else None
114	    value = pacing.get("default_clip_sec") if isinstance(pacing, dict) else None
115	    return float(value) if isinstance(value, (int, float)) else None

118	# Effects whose animation arrays should be sourced from the theme/effect
119	# defaults.json — never from the LLM brief output. The brief's job is content,
120	# not styling. New branded effects should be added here as they're authored.
121	_BRANDED_EFFECT_IDS = frozenset({"section-hook", "art-card", "resource-card", "cta-card"})
122	
123	
124	def _drop_brand_animation_overrides(effect_id: str, params: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
125	    """Remove entrance/exit/sustain keys from params for branded effects.
126	
127	    For branded effects, the theme's effect defaults.json is authoritative. The LLM
128	    brief sometimes proposes entrance/exit arrays anyway; we silently drop them
129	    rather than threading them through to the timeline.
130	    """
131	    if effect_id not in _BRANDED_EFFECT_IDS or not params:
132	        return params
133	    return {key: value for key, value in params.items() if key not in {"entrance", "exit", "sustain"}}
134	
135	
136	def arrangement_uses_generative_visuals(arrangement: dict[str, Any], pool: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
137	    generative_ids = {
138	        entry["id"]
139	        for entry in pool.get("entries", [])
140	        if isinstance(entry, dict) and isinstance(entry.get("id"), str) and entry.get("kind") == "generative"
141	    }
142	    for clip in arrangement.get("clips", []):
143	        visual_source = clip.get("visual_source") if isinstance(clip, dict) else None
144	        if isinstance(visual_source, dict) and visual_source.get("pool_id") in generative_ids:
145	            return True
146	    return False

149	def load_scenes(path: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
150	    data = load_json(path)
151	    if not isinstance(data, list):
152	        raise SystemExit(f"Expected a JSON list in {path}")
153	    return data

155	def load_transcript_segments(path: Path | None) -> list[dict[str, Any]] | None:
156	    if path is None:
157	        return None
158	    data = load_json(path)
159	    segments = data.get("segments") if isinstance(data, dict) else data
160	    if not isinstance(segments, list):
161	        raise SystemExit(f"Expected transcript segments in {path}")
162	    return segments
