> DEVELOPER

No code. ~600 words. Pure design.

I'm designing an orchestrator for the ArtAgents toolkit (file-based creative-work toolkit) that produces an **iteration video**: a walkthrough of every iteration that fed a finished creative artifact (image, video, song). The killer demo:

> Open a finished video. One click → "show me the 47 runs that led here, in order, with the prompt diffs and the sibling variants we considered at each step." That output is itself a video.

**Existing primitives in this toolkit:**

- `run` = one CLI invocation, lives in `runs/<slug>/`, has `run.json` with `run_id`, `thread_id`, `parent_run_ids` (causal), `input_artifacts`, `output_artifacts`.
- `thread` = a pursuit. Has `run_ids`, `canonical_run_id`.
- `variant_group` (in design) = N sibling artifacts produced from one attempt, with optional `chosen_variant`.
- Existing executors include `builtin.cut` (produces `hype.timeline.json` + `hype.assets.json`), `builtin.render` (timeline → mp4), `builtin.transcribe`, `builtin.understand` (audio/visual/video dispatcher), `builtin.generate_image`, `builtin.validate`. Plus orchestrators `builtin.hype`, `builtin.event_talks`, `builtin.thumbnail_maker`, `builtin.logo_ideas`.
- Elements/effects/transitions exist as composable render building blocks.

**Design `builtin.iteration_video`:**

1. **Inputs.** What does this orchestrator take?
   - A thread id? A finished artifact path (and infer thread)? A timeline file (and walk its `provenance.contributing_runs`)?
   - Filters: only chosen variants vs all variants? Time range? Min/max iterations to include?
   - Style: documentary-pace, hype-cut-pace, slow-grid-pace?
   - Output spec (resolution, duration target)?

2. **The two visual tracks problem.** Iterations come in two flavors:
   - **Items** (single artifacts like images, audio clips) — naturally shown as stills or short panning shots, with sibling variants placeable side-by-side as a grid that resolves into the chosen one.
   - **Timelines** (which are themselves multi-clip compositions producing videos) — each iteration is a video that's hard to "show" without playing it. Options: thumbnail strip with playhead, picture-in-picture, scrub through key frames, play the first/last 3 seconds, full playthrough at 4x.
   - How does the orchestrator handle both, and how does it switch modes when a thread mixes the two (e.g. iterate on a logo, then iterate on a video that uses the logo)?

3. **Variants visualization.** When run #7 produced 4 candidates and the user picked #3:
   - Show all 4 in a grid that animates to highlight the chosen?
   - Show only the chosen, with a faint "+3 alternatives" badge?
   - Cut between all 4 quickly then settle on the chosen?
   - User-configurable, with a smart default?

4. **Pacing & length.** A thread of 47 runs at 2 seconds each = 94 seconds. At 5 seconds = 4 minutes. What's the right per-iteration budget? Should pacing dilate when the prompt diff is large (interesting moment) and contract when it's small (refinement)? Should sibling variants get extra time or be tightly cut?

5. **Prompt diffs as a visual element.** Between iteration N and N+1, the brief / prompt usually changed. Surface as:
   - On-screen text overlay diff (red strikethrough + green addition)?
   - Voiceover (TTS the diff)?
   - Subtitle ticker?
   - Skip entirely if "creator" mode wants the visuals to speak for themselves?

6. **Composition.** Which existing executors does this orchestrator chain?
   - `builtin.understand` to summarize each iteration?
   - `builtin.generate_image` for transition cards?
   - A new executor `iteration.assemble` that builds the timeline?
   - `builtin.cut` to wire it up?
   - `builtin.render` to produce the mp4?
   - What's the artifact handoff between them?

7. **The reliability question.** This orchestrator is only as good as the data it reads. If `provenance.contributing_runs` is patchy, if thread membership is wrong, if variants aren't grouped, the video lies. What does the orchestrator do when it encounters bad/missing data:
   - Fail loudly?
   - Best-effort with gaps?
   - Print a `data quality report` before rendering?
   - Refuse to render below a quality threshold?

8. **Existing-orchestrator analogues.** `builtin.hype` produces a hype-cut video from source media; `builtin.event_talks` does talks. How much can `iteration_video` reuse, and where does it need new pieces?

9. **The output.** What does the orchestrator produce besides the mp4?
   - A static HTML "documentation" view as a sibling artifact?
   - The intermediate timeline so the user can edit it?
   - A JSON manifest of which runs were included?

Give me a concrete spec: name, inputs, outputs, the chain of executor calls, the timeline schema it produces, and the three sharpest design tradeoffs you'd flag for the user. Be opinionated.

1	# Examples
2	
3	The `examples/` directory contains committed schema fixtures and small sample
4	briefs. Generated media does not belong here.
5	
6	## Briefs
7	
8	`examples/briefs/` contains human-readable pure-generative briefs that are safe
9	to commit and useful for manual smoke runs:
10	
11	```bash
12	python3 -m artagents --brief examples/briefs/cinematic.txt --out runs/cinematic --render --target-duration 15
13	python3 -m artagents --brief examples/briefs/surreal.txt --out runs/surreal --render --target-duration 15
14	```
15	
16	## Media Fixtures
17	
18	Generate local sample media on demand with:
19	
20	```bash
21	ffmpeg -f lavfi -i testsrc=duration=42:size=1920x1080:rate=30 -c:v libx264 examples/main.mp4
22	ffmpeg -f lavfi -i testsrc=duration=18:size=1280x720:rate=24 -c:v libx264 examples/broll.mp4
23	```
24	
25	Notes:
26	
27	- `main.mp4`, `broll.mp4`, and other generated media are not committed.
28	  Generate them locally when you need a real fixture render.
29	- `hype.timeline.full.json` and `hype.assets.full.json` are schema-only fixtures consumed by the smoke test and `tools/tests/test_schema_contract.py`.
30	- The full fixture `file` paths point at the on-demand media names, but those files do not need to exist for bundle-only smoke checks.
31

    deep: float
    quotability: float

    id: str
    kind: PoolKind
    category: PoolCategory
    duration: float
    scores: PoolScores
    excluded: bool

class PoolEntry(_PoolEntryRequired, total=False):
    asset: str
    src_start: float
    src_end: float
    source_ids: SourceIds
    effect_id: str
    param_schema: dict[str, Any]
    defaults: dict[str, Any]
    meta: dict[str, Any]
    excluded_reason: str | None
    text: str
    speaker: str | None
    quote_kind: str
    motion_tags: list[str]
    mood_tags: list[str]
    subject: str
    camera: str
    intensity: float
    event_label: str
    bed_kind: str
    energy: float

class Pool(TypedDict, total=False):

    source_slug: str

class ArrangementTextOverlay(TypedDict, total=False):
    content: str

ArrangementVisualRole = Literal["primary", "overlay", "stinger"]

class _ArrangementVisualSourceRequired(TypedDict):

    uuid: str
    order: int
    audio_source: ArrangementAudioSource | None
    visual_source: ArrangementVisualSource

class ArrangementClip(_ArrangementClipRequired, total=False):

    validate_pool(pool)
    _write_json(path, pool)

```
def render_concepts(
    *,
    concepts: list[dict[str, Any]],
    layout: dict[str, Path],
    provider: str,
    image_size: str | dict[str, int],
    output_format: str,
    fal_key: str | None,
    dry_run: bool,
) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    ext = _ext_for_format(output_format)
    results: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    for concept in concepts:
        candidate_id = concept["candidate_id"]
        dest = layout["images"] / f"{candidate_id}.{ext}"
        if dry_run or not fal_key:
            generated = _placeholder_image(dest, concept["name"])
        else:
            payload = _fal_payload(provider, concept["prompt"], image_size, output_format)
            submission = submit_fal_job(provider, payload, fal_key)
            result = poll_fal_result(submission, fal_key)
            generated = _save_first_image(result, dest)
            generated["request_id"] = submission.get("request_id")
        results.append({**concept, "generated": generated})
    return results
```

# `builtin.iteration_video` — Design Spec

## Name & one-line purpose
`builtin.iteration_video` — given any finished artifact, render a video walkthrough of the runs that produced it, with prompt diffs and sibling variants surfaced inline.

## Inputs

```
target:           <artifact-path> | <run-id> | <thread-id>     (required, exactly one)
                  Default resolution: artifact -> producing run -> thread.
scope:            chosen-only | all-variants | chosen+badges    (default: chosen+badges)
ancestry:         thread | provenance-graph                    (default: provenance-graph)
                  thread = strict thread.run_ids order
                  provenance-graph = walk parent_run_ids from target backward
time_range:       [iso, iso] | null
max_iterations:   int | null   (if exceeded, downsample by importance)
style:            documentary | hype | grid-contemplative      (default: documentary)
per_iter_budget:  seconds (float) | "auto"                     (default: auto)
diff_surface:     overlay | voiceover | ticker | none          (default: overlay)
output_spec:      { width, height, fps, target_duration }      (default: 1920x1080, 30, derived)
quality_floor:    0.0–1.0  (refuse render if data-quality < floor; default 0.6)
```

Why these defaults: `provenance-graph` over `thread` because threads are a human construct and lie more often than parent_run_ids do. `chosen+badges` because grids-of-grids are visually exhausting. `auto` budget because run counts vary by 50x.

## Outputs

A **variant_group** containing:

1. `iteration.mp4` — the rendered video (canonical artifact).
2. `iteration.timeline.json` — the full hype-style timeline, editable, re-renderable via `builtin.render`.
3. `iteration.manifest.json` — ordered list of `{run_id, role: item|timeline, variants: [...], chosen, prompt_diff_to_prev, allocated_seconds, importance_score}`.
4. `iteration.report.html` — static doc view, sibling artifact, same data.
5. `iteration.quality.json` — what was missing, what was inferred, confidence per iteration.

The mp4 is `chosen_variant`. The HTML is the "I want to read this not watch it" escape hatch.

## Executor chain

```
1. iteration.collect          (NEW)   target -> ordered run list + variant groups + diffs
                                       reads run.json, thread.json, variant_group.json
                                       emits iteration.manifest.json + iteration.quality.json
                                       gates on quality_floor

2. iteration.summarize        (calls builtin.understand per iteration, parallel)
                                       cheap captions for items; for timeline-iters,
                                       a 1-line "what changed in this cut"

3. iteration.score             (NEW)   prompt-diff magnitude + variant-count + summarize
                                       output -> importance_score; drives pacing dilation

4. iteration.assemble          (NEW)   manifest + scores + style -> hype.timeline.json
                                       picks per-iter visual mode (still / grid / pip / scrub)
                                       inserts diff overlays as text elements
                                       inserts transition cards (optionally calls
                                       builtin.generate_image for chapter dividers)

5. builtin.cut                          normalize timeline + asset bundle

6. builtin.render                       -> iteration.mp4
```

`iteration.assemble` is the only piece that needs real new visual logic. Everything else is glue.

## Timeline schema (additions over hype)

The output is a standard `hype.timeline.json` with one extension: each clip carries `iteration_meta`:

```
clips: [
  {
    ...standard hype clip fields (src, in, out, transitions, effects)...
    iteration_meta: {
      run_id, iteration_index, role: "item"|"timeline",
      visual_mode: "still"|"grid"|"pip"|"scrub"|"playthrough_4x",
      variant_grid: [{path, chosen: bool}] | null,
      prompt_diff: { removed: [...], added: [...] } | null,
      importance_score: 0.0–1.0
    }
  }
]
```

Storing it on the clip means: a human can open the timeline, see why each clip is there, edit pacing, re-render — without losing the provenance trail. The render executor ignores `iteration_meta`; it's metadata-as-documentation.

## Mixed-mode handling (items + timelines in one thread)

`iteration.assemble` chooses `visual_mode` per iteration:

- item, single variant -> `still` (Ken Burns by default in documentary style)
- item, multi-variant -> `grid` that resolves to chosen over ~1.2s
- timeline, short (<15s) -> `playthrough_4x`
- timeline, long -> `scrub` (8 keyframes with playhead) for documentary, `pip` (corner-playing while the diff overlay sits center) for hype
- mode transitions get a 0.4s chapter card so the eye knows the visual grammar changed

This is the right place for the orchestrator to be opinionated. Don't expose 12 knobs; pick.

## Pacing

Auto budget: base `B = clamp(target_duration / N, 1.5s, 6s)`. Per-iteration allocation = `B * (0.5 + importance_score)`. Result: 50-iteration refinement threads breeze past tweaks and linger on the moments where the brief actually moved. Variants in `grid` mode get +0.8s, not multiplicative — keeps grids from blowing the budget.

If no `target_duration` given, derive `N * 2.5s` then apply importance dilation.

## Variants

Default: `chosen+badges` — show chosen full-frame with a small "3 alternatives" affordance bottom-right that, in `grid-contemplative` style, expands inline for 1.5s. In `hype`, badges only. In `documentary`, expand on iterations where `importance_score > 0.7`. This is the "smart default" for question 3 — don't make the user choose, do the right thing per style.

## Prompt diffs

Default `overlay`: red strikethrough + green addition, lower third, fades 0.3s. `voiceover` is tempting but TTS-of-diffs sounds robotic on small edits ("removed comma, added 'cinematic'") — only enable when `importance_score > 0.6` for that iteration. `ticker` is for grid-contemplative style where the visual is doing the work and you want a reading layer. `none` is creator-cut mode.

## Reliability behavior

`iteration.collect` produces a `data_quality` score in [0,1]:

- 1.0 baseline; subtract for each missing `parent_run_ids`, ungrouped sibling, missing prompt, orphaned artifact
- if score < `quality_floor`: **refuse to render**, emit `iteration.quality.json` + a stub HTML report explaining what's missing and which runs to repair
- if score >= floor but <1.0: render with gaps, mark inferred iterations with a subtle "inferred" corner badge, list them in the report

Failing loud is wrong (data is always patchy). Best-effort silently is wrong (the video lies). The quality floor + visible-inference badge is the right shape.

## Reuse from existing orchestrators

- From `builtin.hype`: timeline shape, transition library, render path. ~70% reuse.
- From `builtin.thumbnail_maker`: grid-layout logic for the `grid` visual mode.
- From `builtin.event_talks`: chapter-card pattern for mode transitions.
- New: `iteration.collect`, `iteration.score`, `iteration.assemble`. Three new executors, all small. `collect` is the only one with non-trivial logic and it's pure data plumbing.

---

## Three sharpest tradeoffs

**1. Provenance-graph vs thread ordering.** Threads are what users *think* in; provenance graphs are what actually happened. They diverge constantly — sidequests, abandoned branches, runs pulled in from other threads. Choosing `provenance-graph` as default means the video sometimes shows runs the user forgot belonged to this artifact. That's either a feature (honest) or a bug (confusing). **Recommendation: provenance-graph default, with `iteration.report.html` clearly labeling which runs were "in-thread" vs "pulled in by ancestry."** If users hate it, flip the default — but find out first.

**2. Timeline iterations are second-class no matter what you do.** A timeline-producing iteration is a 90-second video; you have ~3 seconds to show it. Every visual mode (`scrub`, `pip`, `playthrough_4x`) is a compromise. The honest answer is that for thread-of-timelines work (e.g. iterating on a hype cut 20 times), this orchestrator's output is *navigation*, not *viewing* — a trailer for the iteration history, with the HTML report as the real artifact. **Recommendation: be explicit in docs that mixed/timeline-heavy threads produce a "guided index" not a "watch-through," and lean harder on the HTML output for those cases.** Don't pretend the video is sufficient.

**3. Quality-floor refusal is going to make people angry the first time it fires.** Users will run this on a thread that *feels* complete, get a refusal, and bounce. But the alternative — silently rendering a video that misorders or omits half the runs — destroys trust in the artifact permanently. **Recommendation: ship with `quality_floor: 0.6` (forgiving), make the refusal screen genuinely actionable (list the 3 specific runs missing parent_run_ids with the exact command to backfill them), and add `--force` for the "I know, just render it" escape.** The refusal has to feel like a helpful editor, not a bureaucrat.

## Multi-Output Executors & Variant Handling Inventory

### 1. **generate_image Executor** 
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/executors/generate_image/run.py:1-431`

**(a) How it's invoked:**
- Supports `--n N` flag (line 408: `add("--n", type=int, default=1, help="Images per prompt.")`), default=1, range 1-10 (lines 168-170)
- Accepts multiple prompts via `--prompt` (repeatable) or `--prompts-file` (text/JSON/JSONL)
- When N>1, generates N variants from a single prompt

**(b) Where outputs land:**
- Images written to `--out-dir` (default: `output/gpt-image`, line 415)
- Per-prompt manifest at `--manifest` (default: `output/gpt-image/manifest.json`, line 416)

**(c) Output naming:**
- Format: `{job_index:03d}-{slugified_prompt}.{ext}` when N=1 (line 236)
- Format: `{stem}-{i}.{ext}` for i in 1..N when N>1 (line 239)
- Example: `001-ascending-triad.png`, `001-ascending-triad-1.png`, `001-ascending-triad-2.png`

**(d) Per-output metadata:**
Manifest structure (lines 305-373):
```python
manifest: list[dict] = [
    {
        "prompt": str,
        "request": {model, n, size, quality, ...},  # Request params except prompt
        "outputs": [list of file paths],           # All N images for this prompt
        "usage": {api usage},                       # Shared across all N
        "created": timestamp,
    }
]
```
- No per-output metadata (image 1 vs 2 within same prompt job are indistinguishable in manifest)
- Audit trail per-output (lines 341-356): each image registered separately with `audit.register_asset()` under same prompt parent

### 2. **logo_ideas Orchestrator**
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/orchestrators/logo_ideas/run.py:1-508`

**(a) How it's invoked:**
- Single orchestrator call with `--count N` (default 9, max 64, lines 77, 109-110)
- Uses 1 Fireworks call (to generate N concept prompts) + N individual fal jobs (one per concept, **not** one fal job with N=count)
- Loops over N concepts, submitting each individually (lines 361-372 in `render_concepts()`)

**(b) Where outputs land:**
Root dir structure:
```
{--out}/
  logo-plan.json          (line 440)
  concepts.json           (line 460)
  prompts.json            (line 461-472)
  grid.jpg                (line 486, assembled from all N images)
  logo-manifest.json      (line 498)
  images/
    logo-001.png          (line 363: candidate_id format)
    logo-002.png
    ...
    logo-NNN.png
```

**(c) Output naming:**
- `candidate_id`: `logo-{index:03d}` (line 223, line 362)
- File: `{candidate_id}.{ext}` (line 363, where ext is png/jpg/webp)

**(d) Per-output metadata (in logo-manifest.json):**
```python
{
  "candidates": [
    {
      "candidate_id": "logo-001",
      "index": 1,
      "name": "Ascending Triad",
      "rationale": "1-sentence why",
      "prompt": "the actual prompt for this one concept",
      "generated": {
        "path": str,
        "source_url": str,
        "width": int, "height": int,
        "bytes": int,
        "content_type": str,
        "request_id": str,  # fal request ID
      }
    },
    ...
  ],
  "count": 9,
  "provider": "z-image" | "gpt-image",
  "grid": {
    "path": str,
    "image_count": 9,
    "cols": 3, "rows": 3
  },
  "mode": "run" | "dry-run"
}
```
- **Sibling relationship**: All candidates are siblings under same orchestrator run; `grid` shows they form a visual group
- **Prompt per-output**: Stored per candidate (line 469: `"prompt": c["prompt"]`)
- **Quality/scores**: None today; fal returns request_id only

### 3. **cut Executor (hype cut planning)**
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/executors/cut/run.py:1-1219`

**(a) Does it produce multiple timeline siblings?**
- **No** (not today). Produces exactly ONE timeline per run: `hype.timeline.json`
- No `--variants` or `--n-cuts` mode (lines 44-81 show CLI args; no variant parameter)
- Can **resume** an existing timeline (line 45: `--timeline`) but doesn't generate alternates

**(b) Output structure:**
- Single timeline per run + assets registry + metadata
- Timeline references pool entries by `pool_id` (line 559); pool entries can have `kind: "generative"` for render effects
- Arrangement layer (lines 700-826) selects which pool entries to use via explicit clip declarations—this is the "decision" layer, not variants

### 4. **render Executor (Remotion)**
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/executors/render/run.py:1-450`

**(a) Does it loop over N timelines?**
- **No**. Strictly one-to-one: one timeline → one video
- No loop, no `--variants` mode (lines 419-431 show CLI args; only `--timeline` singular)

**(b) Multi-output via composition?**
- Composition ID is configurable (line 424: `--composition`, default `TimelineComposition`)
- Single video output per call

### 5. **Real Example: runs/artagents_logo_v2/**

### 9. **Manifest File Convention**
**Pattern seen across:**
- `generate_image`: writes `manifest.json` (list of prompt jobs + outputs per job)
- `logo_ideas`: writes `logo-manifest.json` (list of candidates + generated image per candidate)

**Common structure:**
```json
{
  "version": 1,
  "mode": "run" | "dry-run",
  "count": N,
  "candidates" | "outputs" | "jobs": [array of variant objects],
  "grid": {path, image_count, ...} | undefined,
  ...
}
```

**What's missing:**
- No standard field for "variant_group_id"
- No standard field for "chosen_one_id" or "is_chosen"
- No pointers to per-variant seeds, quality scores, or audit decisions

### 10. **The "ran 4 times" pattern**
**Observed in logo_ideas:**
- Conceptually: "Run 1 orchestrator call" = "Generate 9 concepts + render 9 images"
- Implementation: "1 Fireworks call (batch) + 9 fal calls (serial per-concept loop)"
- **Not** "9 separate generate_image runs with --count 1"

**Compare to generate_image:**
- generate_image: "1 call with --n=9" produces 9 images in parallel (OpenAI API handles it)
- logo_ideas: "1 call with --count=9" does 1 concept generation + 9 sequential image renders

## Design Gaps (What's Missing Today)

For a "variant group" primitive to work, you need:

| Concept | Where Stored | Example | Status |
|---------|--------------|---------|--------|
| **Group ID** | Manifest + audit | `variant_group_id: "artagents_logo_v2"` | ❌ Missing |
| **Sibling enumeration** | Manifest (index) | `index: 1..9` | ✓ Implicit (index field) |
| **Per-variant prompt** | Manifest | `prompt: "..."` | ✓ (logo_ideas does this) |
| **Per-variant seed** | Manifest | `seed: 12345` | ❌ Not captured |
| **Quality/scoring** | Manifest | `scores: {lq_model: 0.8}` | ❌ Not captured |
| **Chosen one marker** | Manifest + audit | `is_chosen: true` or audit decision | ❌ Missing |
| **Audit linkage** | Manifest | `decision_id: "..."` pointer | ❌ Not linked |
| **Output paths** | Manifest | `path: "images/logo-001.png"` | ✓ (but absolute; should be relative) |

**Key insight:** Logo_ideas is **already** multi-output-aware (9 candidates array in manifest), but:
- No mechanism to mark "the user picked #3"
- No variant_group_id linking it to sibling runs (v1, v2, v3 are separate root dirs today)
- No cross-reference to the audit ledger decision

## Concrete Recommendations for Your Variant Group Design

1. **Manifest amendment:** Add to all manifest files:
   ```json
   {
     "variant_group": {
       "id": "str (uuid or run-dir-basename)",
       "count": 9,
       "siblings": ["path/to/sibling_run1", "path/to/sibling_run2"],  // optional
     },
     "candidates": [
       {
         "index": 1,
         "candidate_id": "logo-001",
         "chosen": false  // or null; set to true when user picks
       }
     ]
   }
   ```

2. **Audit integration:** When user chooses variant N:
   ```python
   audit.register_decision(
       stage="variant_selection",
       label=f"Selected {candidate_id} from {variant_group_id}",
       selected=[asset_id_of_chosen],
       rejected=[asset_ids_of_others],
       metadata={"variant_group_id": variant_group_id}
   )
   ```

3. **Output path convention:** Make relative paths in manifest so variant groups can be moved/copied:
   - Store: `images/logo-001.png` not `/full/path/to/images/logo-001.png`

4. **Generator contract:** All multi-output executors should:
   - Write a manifest with a `variant_group` block
   - Include per-output `candidate_id` or `index`
   - Include per-output `prompt` (if applicable)
   - Not assume N=1; support N>1

5. **Selection UI integration:** When rendering a variant group for human review:
   - Show grid.jpg or carousel of all N
   - Expose a "choose" button that writes back to manifest + audit decision
   - Query audit ledger to show "decision made at [time] by [user]"

No code. ~700 words. Pure design.

I'm designing `builtin.iteration_video` for the ArtAgents toolkit — an orchestrator that produces a video walkthrough of every iteration that fed a finished creative artifact. The toolkit is invoked by **LLM agents** (like Claude Code), not humans typing commands. The agent has to be able to direct the iteration video without writing custom code, just by passing flags and reading documentation.

**The killer demo:** open a finished artifact → "show me the 47 runs that fed this, in order, with sibling variants and the prompt diffs."

**What I have so far** (from prior design rounds):
- A thread layer auto-attributes runs to creative pursuits.
- `output_artifacts` carry `role: variant|ancillary|manifest`, `group`, `group_index`, `variant_meta`.
- A `selections.jsonl` log per thread captures keep/discard decisions.
- The orchestrator chains: `iteration.collect` → `iteration.summarize` (calls `builtin.understand`) → `iteration.score` → `iteration.assemble` → `builtin.cut` → `builtin.render`.
- A `style` flag with three presets: `documentary`, `hype`, `grid-contemplative`.
- Visual modes per iteration: `still`, `grid`, `pip`, `scrub`, `playthrough_4x` — chosen by a hardcoded decision table from (iteration shape × style).

**The new question I need you to think through:**

The toolkit will produce iteration videos for *wildly* different threads. Examples:

1. **Logo design thread** — 30 image-generation runs, each producing 4 candidates, picking one each time, occasional brief tweaks.
2. **Hype-cut thread** — 12 timeline iterations producing 12 different 90-second hype videos cut from the same source footage. The actual *content* is video.
3. **Song iteration thread** — 8 audio runs producing variants of a 2-minute track. The artifact is sound; the iterations are tonal/structural.
4. **Storyboard-to-animation thread** — heterogeneous: starts with text briefs, becomes storyboard images, becomes Moirae screenplay, becomes animated video. Modalities change *within* the thread.
5. **Music video thread** — cross-modal: iterating on a song AND the cover art AND the music video together; three sub-pursuits intertwined.
6. **Code-art thread** — generating ShaderToy / Processing sketches; iterations are video clips of running code, plus the source code itself.
7. **3D model thread** — iterations are .obj/.glb files; preview is a turntable render.
8. **Voice/narration thread** — iterations are TTS variants of a script; previews are waveforms + playback.
9. **Documentary essay thread** — markdown/text iterations; iteration history is fundamentally textual.

**Pressure-test the design with these questions — be opinionated:**

1. **Is the hardcoded decision table (iteration shape × style → visual mode) too rigid for this range?** When the agent encounters a song-iteration thread, the decision table has no row for "audio variant." When it encounters cross-modal, it has no notion of "two pursuits in parallel." What's a more flexible primitive — a registry of *modality renderers* the agent can mix and match? A capability declaration on each artifact (`{kind, preview_modes: ["still", "audio_waveform", "play_excerpt"]}`)? A pluggable "renderer" element type that maps `(artifact_kind, available_previews) → clip_spec`?

2. **How does the agent discover what's possible?** If we add modality renderers (image-grid, audio-waveform, text-diff, 3D-turntable, code-scroll), how does the agent learn they exist? Through `iteration_video inspect`? A `--list-renderers` subcommand? Auto-detection from artifact kinds in the thread?

3. **Cross-modal coherence — the hardest case.** A music-video thread iterates on a song, its cover art, and the video. The honest iteration video has three intertwined tracks. Options:
   - One linear iteration video that alternates focus (song → art → video → song...).
   - Three parallel iteration videos, one per modality, with cross-references.
   - A "split-screen" mode where the iteration video shows all three evolving in parallel.
   - A "thread per pursuit, then a meta-video that stitches" pattern.
   - Something else.
   What's the right primitive, and what's the agent gesture to invoke it?

4. **Modality-appropriate audio bed.** The iteration video itself plays audio. For a song-iteration thread, the audio bed should *be* the iterations (cross-fading between variants). For a logo thread, silence-or-music-bed. For a video-edit thread, the source audio. How does this get decided — automatic from dominant artifact kind, or explicit? Is there a default that works 80% of the time without the agent thinking about it?

5. **How does the agent provide creative direction without code?** A creator might want "make the variant grids feel like a contact sheet from a darkroom" or "between iterations, fade through black with a typewriter sound." This is real direction. Options:
   - Style presets cover the common cases; everything else needs custom timeline editing.
   - A `--direction "..."` natural-language flag the orchestrator passes to `iteration.assemble` to influence transitions, color grade, audio bed.
   - A `theme` (existing concept in this toolkit's `themes/` directory) the iteration video respects.
   - The agent edits the intermediate `iteration.timeline.json` directly with `builtin.cut`-style ops.
   What's the right ergonomic for "I have a creative opinion, here's how to express it"?

6. **Heterogeneous-modality threads (storyboard → animation).** When modality changes mid-thread, the iteration video has to switch visual languages. Should the orchestrator detect modality boundaries and insert chapter cards explicitly ("Phase 1: brief", "Phase 2: storyboard", "Phase 3: animation")? Or let the visual changes speak for themselves? What's the default?

7. **Modalities the toolkit doesn't natively understand.** When the agent encounters a `kind: glb` artifact and there's no 3D renderer, what happens? Skip silently? Render as filename card? Fall back to a generic "artifact icon + filename + modification time"? Pluggable fallback?

8. **The agent's escape hatch.** When the orchestrator gets it wrong, the agent needs to fix it. The `iteration.timeline.json` is an editable hype timeline — but editing it requires understanding hype's schema. Is there a higher-level edit gesture, like `iteration_video edit <run-id> --visual-mode pip` to override one iteration's treatment? Or does the agent just regenerate with different flags?

Recommend a concrete redesign:
- The new primitive (modality renderer registry? capability declarations? something else?)
- Where it lives in the codebase (a new `artagents/modalities/` directory? extension to `elements/`? per-executor opt-in?)
- The exact agent-facing flags and discovery surface
- The decision: replace the hardcoded decision table or augment it
- The default behaviors that make the right thing happen without thought, for each of the 9 example threads above

Be opinionated. If "iteration video" should split into multiple orchestrators by modality, say so. If the whole frame is wrong, say so. Don't hedge.

> AGENT

# Iteration Video: A Redesign

## The frame is mostly right; the decision table is wrong

Keep `builtin.iteration_video` as a single orchestrator. Splitting by modality is the obvious move and the wrong one — half of real threads are heterogeneous (storyboard→animation, music video) and the agent shouldn't have to pick `iteration_video_audio` vs `iteration_video_image` before it knows what's in the thread. One orchestrator, polymorphic over modality.

Kill the hardcoded `(iteration shape × style → visual mode)` table. Replace it with a **modality renderer registry** plus **artifact capability declarations**. The table becomes a fallback for the trivial case (image-grid threads) and a tiebreaker, not the spine.

## The primitive: ModalityRenderer

A `ModalityRenderer` is a pure descriptor — a tiny manifest, not code-the-agent-writes. Each one declares:

- `kinds`: artifact kinds it consumes (`["png", "jpg"]`, `["wav", "mp3"]`, `["glb", "obj"]`, `["md", "txt"]`)
- `clip_modes`: what it can produce (`still`, `grid`, `pip`, `scrub`, `playthrough_4x`, `waveform`, `excerpt_3s`, `turntable`, `text_diff`, `code_scroll`)
- `default_clip_mode_for(iteration_shape, style)`: the renderer's own decision table, scoped to the modality it knows
- `produces_audio`: whether its clips carry meaningful audio (drives audio-bed selection)
- `cost_hint`: cheap/medium/expensive (drives whether `playthrough_4x` is reasonable for 47 iterations)

Lives at `artagents/modalities/` as sibling to `elements/` and `executors/`. Each renderer is one file: `image_grid.py`, `audio_waveform.py`, `video_pip.py`, `text_diff.py`, `model_turntable.py`, `code_scroll.py`, `generic_card.py` (the always-available fallback).

## Capability declarations on artifacts

Extend `output_artifacts` with:

```
{ kind: "wav", preview_modes: ["waveform", "excerpt_3s", "full_play"], duration: 124.0 }
```

Producers (the executors that emit artifacts) declare what previews are tractable. The renderer registry resolves `(kind, available_previews)` to a renderer at assembly time. This is the missing capability layer — currently the orchestrator has to *guess* what's renderable.

## Agent discovery surface

Three flags, one inspect command. That's it.

- `iteration_video inspect <thread>` — prints: detected modalities, dominant modality, suggested style, available renderers per modality, default clip-mode per iteration, estimated runtime. **This is the agent's primary tool.** Read once, then drive.
- `--renderers image=grid,audio=waveform,video=pip` — override per-modality renderer choice
- `--clip-mode <run-id>=pip` — override one iteration's treatment (the escape hatch from Q8; cheaper than editing the timeline)
- `--direction "contact sheet from a darkroom"` — natural language, passed verbatim to `iteration.assemble` which uses it to bias transitions, grade, and audio-bed prompt. This is the right ergonomic for Q5; presets handle the 80% case, themes handle the branded case, `--direction` handles the "I have a creative opinion" case without forcing the agent into the hype timeline schema.

The `themes/` directory wins automatically when a theme is set on the thread — no flag needed. Theme > direction > style preset > defaults.

## Cross-modal coherence (Q3)

The right primitive is **pursuit tracks**, not split-screen. The thread layer already attributes runs to creative pursuits — extend that to attribute to *sub-pursuits* (song / cover / video). The orchestrator emits one iteration video by default with **modality chapters** (Q6: yes, explicit chapter cards, the modality shift is too jarring otherwise). Add `--mode parallel` to emit N videos plus a meta-stitch, and `--mode interleaved` for the alternating-focus cut. Default: chaptered single video. The split-screen idea is a trap — it looks cool in mockups and is unwatchable for 47 iterations.

## Audio bed (Q4)

Automatic from dominant modality, with a clean rule:
- If `produces_audio` renderer covers >40% of clip duration → **iterations are the audio bed** (cross-fade variants). Songs, hype cuts, voice.
- Else if theme declares an audio bed → use it.
- Else → silence with subtle room tone. **Not generative music.** Generative beds age badly and fight the content.

Override with `--audio-bed iterations|theme|silence|<path>`.

## Unknown modalities (Q7)

`generic_card` renderer is always the registered fallback: filename, kind badge, mtime, prompt excerpt if available, 2.5s hold. Never silently skip — a missing iteration in a walkthrough is a lie about the creative process. Log a warning that a better renderer would help.

## Defaults for the 9 threads

1. **Logo** — `image_grid`, doc style, silence bed, no chapters
2. **Hype cuts** — `video_pip` showing prior vs new cut, hype style, source audio bed
3. **Song** — `audio_waveform` + `excerpt_3s`, doc style, **iterations as bed**
4. **Storyboard→animation** — heterogeneous, chaptered, renderer switches per chapter, silence→source-audio at the animation chapter
5. **Music video** — chaptered single video by default; `inspect` surfaces `--mode parallel` as suggestion
6. **Code-art** — `code_scroll` + `video_pip` of the running sketch, grid-contemplative, source audio if any
7. **3D** — `model_turntable`, doc style, silence
8. **Voice** — `audio_waveform` + transcript overlay, **iterations as bed**
9. **Documentary essay** — `text_diff`, grid-contemplative, silence with typewriter accents if `--direction` mentions it

## The decision table: replaced, not augmented

Each renderer owns its own scoped decision table. The orchestrator's only global decision is **which renderer per modality**, resolved from capability declarations. This is the correct factoring — global tables that try to know about every modality become unmaintainable exactly at the point the toolkit gets interesting.

Relevant existing paths to extend: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/elements/`, `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/executors/`, `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/themes/`. New: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/modalities/`.

## 1. Role & Mission
Your job is to drive the megaplan workflow through the CLI until the run finishes or a defined breakpoint requires the outer conversation.

Always follow these priorities, in order:
1. The latest user direction relayed through notes or resume messages.
2. The live CLI state from `megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. The workflow and breakpoint rules in this template.
4. Your own memory of earlier turns.

Always do these things:
- Operate through the `megaplan` CLI only. Do not call workers or agents directly.
- Keep the outer conversation clean. Do not ask for routine confirmation.
- Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for routing. If memory and CLI state disagree, trust CLI state.
- Follow `orchestrator_guidance` after `gate` unless you have a concrete reason to disagree after checking plan artifacts or repository evidence yourself.
- Treat user notes as authoritative.

Never do these things:
- Do not run the workflow manually outside the CLI.
- Do not skip required phases for the selected robustness level.
- Do not emit a breakpoint unless one of the breakpoint rules below says to.

## 2. Startup
The `megaplan` command is NOT on PATH directly. The verified launcher for this environment is:
    PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan ...
Use this exact prefix for every megaplan invocation in this run. Do not try bare `megaplan`.

Start the run like this:
1. AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG expands to empty string (raw_config.execution.auto_approve is explicitly set in user config; do not pass --auto-approve).
2. ROBUSTNESS_FLAG expands to `--robustness standard` (raw_config.execution.robustness is NOT explicitly set).
3. Run this exact init command (note --profile, --mode metaplan, --output, --from-doc):
    PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan init \
      --project-dir [REDACTED] \
      --robustness standard \
      --profile standard \
      --mode metaplan \
      --output docs/sprint-thread-layer.md \
      --from-doc docs/design-thread-layer.md \
      "Plan a focused 2-week (10 working day) sprint to ship the thread/variant/iteration-video layer for ArtAgents. Full design in docs/design-thread-layer.md (imported via --from-doc); every Settled Decision is a hard constraint. Produce docs/sprint-thread-layer.md with day-by-day deliverables, critical path, explicit risks with mitigations, per-day acceptance criteria, and resolutions to the design doc's Open Questions. Week 1 (days 1-5) = data layer (must ship SD-008 metadata, SD-013 to SD-017 variants, SD-037 tests, SD-009 brief snapshotting). Week 2 (days 6-10) = iteration video MVP per SD-020 to SD-029 plus 3 modality renderers from SD-035 (image_grid, audio_waveform, generic_card). SD-036 sprint cuts are out of scope. Include a one-page exec summary at the top, integration test plan, and a dogfood plan against runs/artagents_logo_v3."
4. Capture the returned plan name.
5. Output `PLAN_NAME: <name>` on its own line immediately after init and before any `BREAKPOINT:` or `COMPLETE:`.
6. Run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan status --plan <name>`.
7. From then on, use that plan name for every command.

At startup and after every later resume:
- Read `state`, `next_step`, and `valid_next`.
- If `notes_count > 0`, read the full `notes` array before acting. Do not track note cursors or indexes; always read the full array.
- Treat all notes as context. If the newest note changes direction, treat that note as the new intent and decide whether to continue, revise, replan, or break out.
- If `active_step` is present, treat it as an in-flight phase marker rather than a completed step. Use `last_step` for the most recent completed phase.

## 3. Phase Routing by Robustness
Use the workflow below exactly.

Standard robustness:
- `init -> prep -> plan -> critique -> gate`
- Then follow the gate decision tree below.
- `prep` may return `skip: true`; do not skip the command yourself.
- When you reach `gated`, run `finalize`.
- Then run `execute`.
- Then run `review`.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, the workflow becomes `finalized -> execute -> review` again until review passes or the CLI reaches its cap.

Gate decision tree:
- Condition 1: `gate_unset`
  Trigger: state is `critiqued` and `valid_next` includes `gate`.
  Action: run `megaplan gate --plan <name>`.
- Condition 2: `gate_iterate`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `ITERATE`, so `valid_next` includes `revise` but not `override force-proceed`.
  Action: run `megaplan revise --plan <name>`, then continue back through `critique` and `gate`.
- Condition 3: `gate_escalate`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `ESCALATE`, so `valid_next` offers `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`.
  Action: stop with `BREAKPOINT: GATE_ESCALATE`.
- Condition 4: `gate_proceed_blocked`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `PROCEED`, but preflight still blocked execution, so state stays `critiqued` and `valid_next` offers `revise` plus `override force-proceed`.
  Action: do not finalize yet. Use `orchestrator_guidance` plus `preflight_results` to fix the blocking checks through `revise`. On iteration 1, the guidance text may stay generic, so inspect `preflight_results` yourself before revising. If the blocker cannot be resolved safely without a user decision, stop with `BREAKPOINT: GATE_BLOCKED`.
- Condition 5: `gate_proceed`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `PROCEED` and preflight passed, so state becomes `gated`.
  Action: run `megaplan finalize --plan <name>`.

Review routing:
- If `review` succeeds, the run is done.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, the CLI moves back to `finalized` with `next_step` set to `execute`. Run `execute` again, then `review` again.
- This rework loop is capped by the CLI at 3 `needs_rework` cycles.

## 4. After Every Phase
After every phase command, immediately run:
`PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan status --plan <name>`

Then do all of the following before choosing the next command:
- Re-read `state`, `next_step`, and `valid_next`.
- If `notes_count > 0`, read the full `notes` array.
- Check whether the newest note changes direction before the next CLI call.
- If the last phase was `gate`, treat `orchestrator_guidance` as a literal routing hint.
- If the phase response and `status` disagree, trust `status`.
- If the next move is unclear, prefer the explicit `valid_next` list over your own reconstruction of the state machine.

## 5. Breakpoints
Use a breakpoint only for the cases in this section. Format every breakpoint exactly like this:

`BREAKPOINT: <type>`
`Plan: <name>`
`State: <state>`
`Summary: <short reason>`
`Context: <artifacts, warnings, or the exact user decision needed>`

Breakpoint types and triggers:
- `GATE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: gate recommends `ESCALATE`, or `valid_next` offers `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`.
- `GATE_BLOCKED`
  Trigger: you are in the `gate_proceed_blocked` branch and need the outer user to decide between more revision and `override force-proceed`.
- `EXECUTE_APPROVAL`
  Trigger: `finalize` succeeded in review mode and `execute` now requires explicit approval. (NOTE: This run is auto-approve, so this should not fire.)
- `PHASE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: a non-execute phase still fails after the required retry, or it returns unusable output twice.
- `EXECUTE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: `execute` reaches the no-progress cap, or repeated blocking/timeouts prevent forward progress.

## 6. Safeguards
Non-execute safeguards:
- If any non-execute phase fails, returns unusable output, or clearly lands in a bad session state, retry that same phase once with `--fresh`.
- If the exact same error appears twice in a row for the same phase, the next retry must use `--fresh`.
- If the phase still fails after the required fresh retry, stop with `BREAKPOINT: PHASE_ESCALATE`.

Execute safeguards:
- Count whether each `execute` call creates forward progress.
- Forward progress means new completed tasks, a completed batch, or a state advance.
- If you hit 3 consecutive `execute` attempts without forward progress, stop with `BREAKPOINT: EXECUTE_ESCALATE`.
- If `execute` times out or is blocked, retry up to that same 3-attempt cap.

Review safeguards:
- `needs_rework` is a normal workflow branch, not a phase failure.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, do not break out. Follow the rework loop and run `execute` again from `finalized`.
- If `review` returns blocked, treat that as a review-phase problem and apply the non-execute retry rule.

Notes and interruption safeguards:
- If the outer skill kills and relaunches you, start fresh with no reliable memory from the prior run.
- After any note injection or relaunch, run `megaplan status --plan <name>` immediately, read the full `notes` array, and treat all notes as context.

## 7. Resume Protocol
When the outer conversation resumes you:
1. Re-read the message carefully.
2. Run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. Read the full `notes` array.
4. Resume from the current CLI state and `next_step`, not from memory.

## 8. Execution Details
Finalize and execute:
- After `gated`, run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan finalize --plan <name>`.
- In auto-approve mode (this run), run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive`.

Per-batch execution:
- If the execution briefing or CLI response indicates per-batch execution, continue batch by batch.
- Use `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan progress --plan <name>` between batch executions.

## 9. Overrides & Plan Editing
- `megaplan override add-note` from any active state.
- `megaplan override force-proceed` from `critiqued` routes to finalize.
- `megaplan override replan` from `critiqued`, `gated`, or `finalized`.
- `megaplan step add/remove/move` while in `planned`, `critiqued`, `gated`, or `finalized`.

## 10. Completion Format
When the workflow completes, return exactly this shape:

`COMPLETE: megaplan run finished`
`Plan: <name>`
`Final state: <state>`
`Summary: <outcome>`
`Artifacts: <key files or reports — must include docs/sprint-thread-layer.md>`
`Follow-up: <only if something remains>`

> AGENT

I'll start by initializing the megaplan run.

1) Day 3 is overpacked. Auto-attribute (5-branch decision per SD-006 including lineage inference), prefix lines (SD-018), 5 CLI subcommands (thread new/list/show/archive/reopen), lifecycle (SD-007), and lazy reaper (SD-033) is realistically 8-10 hours of focused work. The only buffer is Day 10 shared with dogfood. Either (a) move the lazy reaper and one CLI subcommand (archive/reopen) to Day 4 morning, or (b) explicitly mark Day 3 as a 'risk day' with a named cut-list if it slips past 6pm.

2) Documentation timing is wrong in Step 4. SKILL.md paragraph + 'executors inspect' / 'orchestrators inspect' footer (SD-038) are scheduled for Day 5, but agents will start seeing the [thread] prefix on Day 3. The footer and SKILL.md paragraph must land Day 3 alongside the prefix lines so an agent reading tool output on Day 3 has docs that explain what they're seeing. Only docs/threads.md (the longer reference) belongs Day 5.

3) Add a 'Frozen Schema Review' checkpoint at end-of-Day-2. Once SD-008 fields ship, any change requires SD-039 migration support. The plan should make this an explicit milestone (literally a line in Day 2 acceptance: 'schema_version=1 frozen; any later change requires migration helper + .bak rotation test added before the change merges').

4) _run_upload_youtube bypass at artagents/executors/runner.py:78 short-circuits _run_executor_inner. SD-030's 'no-op when out is unwritable' covers it but the integration test plan must include a test that confirms upload.youtube produces zero thread artifacts and zero errors.

5) Nested-executor env threading is real plumbing not scheduled. SD-030 says 'children skip begin if ARTAGENTS_THREAD_ID env is set'. This requires modifying _run_external_executor's subprocess env at runner.py:258 to thread ARTAGENTS_THREAD_ID through. Schedule on Day 3 (alongside the wrapper expansion) and add a test in tests/test_threads_nested.py that verifies parent->child inheritance.

6) Secret/env-var redaction in cli_args_redacted (SD-008) needs a Day 2 scrub even before SD-041's broader privacy work lands. Basic rule: any CLI arg whose value matches an env-var-style pattern containing KEY/TOKEN/SECRET/PASSWORD gets the value replaced with '***REDACTED***'. Schedule as a 30-min Day 2 task with a unit test.

7) The Risks section is too generic. For each named risk add a one-line concrete mitigation. Specifically: 'Lock contention' -> 'fcntl 30s timeout, error message points at thread gc --force-unlock command'; 'builtin.understand rate limits' -> 'ThreadPoolExecutor max_workers configurable via env var ARTAGENTS_SUMMARIZE_CONCURRENCY default 4, with exponential backoff per call'; 'Schema bug post-Day-2' -> 'SD-039 migration helper added before any field change; .bak rotation test required'.

8) Add a Day 10 'demo milestone' as an acceptance criterion. Not just 'render works' but 'produce a 60-second iteration video against runs/artagents_logo_v3, screen-share it, manual checklist signed off'. The dogfood is the demo.

These are additive to the gate's 3 items already being addressed in revise. Land all 8 (gate's 3 + these 5 not already overlapping) before finalize.
---
[2026-05-03T16:07:58Z] [user]
AGENT UX LAYER GAPS (orchestrator review). The plan currently treats agent UX as deliverables-to-land rather than a UX surface to design. Add an explicit '## Agent UX Surface' subsection to the sprint plan with day-tagged commitments addressing all of the following:

1) TIERED NOTICE/WARN DISCIPLINE. SD-018 names three tiers (info / notice / warn) but the plan must commit to firing rules: Info = every run; Notice = first run of process, gap >1h, cwd change, auto-reopen of archived thread; Warn = brief-novelty against thread history (parsable, rare). Acceptance criterion: 'Warn tier fires <5% of runs in dogfood or the trigger thresholds get tuned before Day 10.' Without this discipline the implementation drifts into warning-spam and agents learn to ignore the lines.

2) [HINT] LINE for suspected manual fan-out (3+ sequential runs of the same executor with no --variants flag) suggesting 'thread group <ids>'. Schedule on Day 4 alongside the [variants] nag. Soft nudge, not warning.

3) [THREAD-HEALTH] SMELL LINE on 'thread list' output (e.g., '12 variant groups, 0 kept' or '3 open threads idle >5d'). Schedule Day 3 with the lifecycle CLI.

4) SKILL.MD ONE-SENTENCE WORDING IS LOAD-BEARING. Every agent will pattern off it. The plan must specify the exact text to land on Day 3, not 'add a paragraph'. Suggested anchor: 'At the start of any session that will produce runs, run python3 -m artagents thread show @active first. The [thread] prefix on every command output is your continuous indicator; if it shows the wrong thread, run thread new or pass --thread @new.' Plan can refine but must commit to the exact wording.

5) OUTPUT-FORMAT STABILITY COMMITMENT. The [thread] and [variants] prefix lines are part of the public CLI contract. Add to Definition of Done: 'prefix line format governed by schema_version; format changes require a major version bump and a transition period.' Agents that pattern-match break silently otherwise.

6) LINE-ORDERING RULE when [thread], [variants], Notice, and Warn all fire on the same run. Specify: stream (stdout for all, before any other output); order (thread, variants, notice, warn, then command output); separator (single blank line between prefix block and command output). Trivial to specify on Day 2, expensive to fix later.

7) ITERATION_VIDEO INSPECT AS PRIMARY DISCOVERY TOOL. The plan treats it as one subcommand among five but it is THE gesture an agent should run before any iteration-video render (per the design's agent-UX reasoning). The SKILL.md text or a separate iteration-video doc must instruct: 'Always run iteration_video inspect <thread> before iteration_video run; it shows detected modalities, chosen renderers, estimated cost, and quality score so you can adjust before committing to a render.'

8) COST ESTIMATE SURFACING per SD-043. iteration_video inspect must print estimated cost (= summarize_calls * model_cost + render_cost_estimate) BEFORE the agent commits to a render. Schedule on Day 6 with iteration.collect (which has the run count) or Day 9 with the orchestrator wiring. Acceptance: 'inspect output includes a Cost section with low/high range and a one-line recommendation.'

9) THE FAILURE-MODE ACCEPTANCE CRITERION (most important). An agent that forgets threads exist will silently lump everything into one giant pile and the killer demo dies. Add to Day 10 dogfood acceptance: 'verify that during dogfood the [thread] prefix appeared on the stdout of every executors run / orchestrators run invocation, that thread show @active was called by the agent before iteration_video, and that no run was attributed to thread @active when a more recent thread had been opened (lineage inference worked).' Make it observable, not assumed.

10) DOCUMENT WHEN EACH TIER FIRES in docs/threads.md so agents reading the doc can predict what they'll see. Day 5 deliverable, scoped to one short table.

These items are load-bearing for adoption: if the agent UX is sloppy, the data the iteration video reads is sloppy, and the demo lies. They are not deferrable to a follow-up sprint.
---
